# Recon findings — the 27 requested changes mapped against the code

Produced by a 14-agent read-only reconnaissance pass over the repository at
commit `7c60e81` ("Cut 0.0.3"). Thirteen of the fourteen areas completed; the
fourteenth (workshop dropdowns on record pages, requirements 9-13) died on an
API error and is the one gap to re-run.

Every claim below was cited to `path:line` by the agent that verified it. Where
an agent reported something as absent, it searched for it and did not find it —
those are findings, not omissions.

---

## Contents

1. File-format description layout — the trace panel's download/export format list (Requirement 8)
2. Android nav + screens (requirements 3, 19, 20)
3. Landing page (frontend/app/page.tsx + frontend/components/hero/*) — section-by-section feature inventory, logos, and institutional branding (Requirements 1, 15, 16)
4. Web walkthrough — /guide, its 19 steps, its motion/layout mechanics, its coverage gaps and its entry points (requirements 2, 3, 19)
5. Recent feature changes — the definitive inventory of what shipped in the last ~15 commits (HEAD 7c60e81 back to 93c70b0, plus the four highly user-visible commits just beyond it), and what the landing page / walkthrough / in-app guide do and do not already say about it
6. Existing feedback system (web /feedback, backend /feedback routes + Prisma Feedback model, Android FeedbackScreen + MasterFeedbackCard) — reconnaissance for Requirements 21, 24, 26 (research/patent-grade user feedback collection)
7. Web sketch upload — Sketches & Prototypes → Upload tab (frontend/components/sketches/**)
8. Web prototypes parity — the PROTOTYPES side of Sketches & Prototypes (Upload tab)
9. Android sketches / trace / measure — requirements 5, 6, 7, 8, 18, 20 on the handset
10. Requirement 14 — "Experience in Years" for Artisan and Designer becoming two dropdowns (Years + Months) on one line
11. Existing analytics / telemetry / behaviour recording (reqs 22-25)
12. Design prototype workshop editing (Requirement 27) — web record page, create flow, backend PATCH, Android twin, house edit-route convention
13. Center of Excellence source — F:/cxa-portal (Requirement 17)

## 1. File-format description layout — the trace panel's download/export format list (Requirement 8)

**Summary.** The format list lives in exactly one web component: `SketchTraceField.tsx`, inside a `const downloads` JSX block (F:/Portal_Development_Designer/frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1619-1735) that is mounted at :2444, inside the "Show more options" advanced disclosure. It is NOT one card per format: it is two separately generated lists over the same table — a full-width `flex flex-wrap` row of five download buttons (:1634-1651), and below it a `<ul>` of five one-line descriptions (:1656-1662). The five entries and their descriptive sentences come from `EXPORT_FORMATS` in `traceExport.ts:141-197` (SVG, PNG, PDF, DXF, EPS). The narrow column is caused by exactly one class: `max-w-prose` on the `<ul>` at SketchTraceField.tsx:1656, which is `max-width: 65ch` resolved against the `text-xs` (12px) font-size set on that same element — roughly 390-420px, while every ancestor container is full width. An Android twin of the list exists (`DwSketchTraceExportCard.kt` + `DwSketchTraceExport.kt`), it carries the same five hint strings verbatim, and it does NOT have the defect: its hint `Text` sits in a `Column(modifier.fillMaxWidth())`.

### What exists today

- The whole download/format section is one JSX constant named `downloads`, declared inside the component body and rendered in exactly one place. Opening tag: `<div className="mt-4 border-t border-line-200 pt-3">`, heading `<span className="field-label">Download a copy to this device</span>`.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1619`
- The format BUTTON row is generated from the table and is full-width: `<div className="mt-1 flex flex-wrap gap-2">` wrapping `{EXPORT_FORMATS.map((entry) => (<button key={entry.id} type="button" className="field-button-secondary" onClick={() => void downloadDerived(entry.id)} disabled={disabled || busy || result === null || file === null}> … {entry.download}</button>))}`. This is the "format info" the requirement refers to.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1634`
- The DESCRIPTION list — the full JSX of one format row, verbatim — is:
<ul className="mt-2 grid max-w-prose gap-1 text-xs leading-5 text-ink-500">
  {EXPORT_FORMATS.map((entry) => (
    <li key={entry.id}>
      <span className="font-medium text-ink-700">{entry.label}</span> — {entry.hint}
    </li>
  ))}
</ul>
SVG is the first `<li>` because it is the first row of `EXPORT_FORMATS`.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1656`
- `downloads` is rendered only at `{downloads}` inside the advanced disclosure body `<div id={advancedId} … className="mt-3 grid gap-4 focus:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-purple-600/40">` — a single-column grid, no column constraint.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:2444`
- The disclosure wrapper is `<div className="mb-3 rounded-md border border-line-200 bg-surface-50 p-3">` and the panel root is `<div id={panelId} className="rounded-lg border border-line-200 bg-surface-50 p-3">` — both full width, no grid columns, no max-width.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1738`
  - also: also :2357 for the disclosure wrapper
- Both host mounts put the panel at full page width inside a `.panel p-4` card — `<SketchTraceField targetLabel={sketchTargetLabel} disabled={disabled} onAttach={onAttachSketch} onAttachSource={onAttachSketchSource} />` in the Sketches tab, and `<SketchTraceField targetLabel={field.label} disabled={disabled} onAttach={attach} />` in the stage form. Neither host is a narrow grid column.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/UploadTabPanel.tsx:220`
  - also: second mount at frontend/components/designworkshop/FieldInput.tsx:1733
- `max-w-prose` is Tailwind's stock utility — `prose: '65ch'` in the default config — and `tailwind.config.ts` / `app/globals.css` contain no `maxWidth` or `prose` override, so it resolves to `max-width: 65ch` unmodified.
  - `frontend/node_modules/tailwindcss/stubs/config.full.js:671`
- The same `hint` string is also shown one-at-a-time under the "Attach as" chips: `<p className="text-xs text-ink-500">{ATTACHABLE_FORMATS.find((e) => e.id === format)?.hint}</p>`. That instance has NO max-width and its parent is `<div className="grid gap-1">`, so it already spans the card — only the download-block list is capped.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:2477`
- `downloadDerived(what: ExportFormatId)` is the handler behind every button; it looks the row up with `EXPORT_FORMATS.find((candidate) => candidate.id === format)` in `exportVectorFile`.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1451`
- Only `SketchTraceField.tsx` renders `EXPORT_FORMATS` anywhere in the frontend; it is the only .tsx file in the repo that mentions DXF. `PrototypeModelField.tsx` has a separate `MODEL_FORMATS` list (GLB/glTF/STL/OBJ/PLY/3MF/FBX/USDZ) rendered as a `<dl className="mt-2 grid gap-1.5">` inside a `<details>` — that one is 3D model files, contains no SVG, and has no max-width cap.
  - `frontend/components/sketches/upload/PrototypeModelField.tsx:426`

### Gaps — what the requirement asks for that is not there

- **The format descriptions do not span the card width. `max-w-prose` on the `<ul>` caps the list at `max-width: 65ch`, and because `text-xs` (font-size 0.75rem = 12px) is set on that SAME element, `ch` resolves against 12px — so the cap is roughly 390-420px rather than the ~650px the same class gives at 16px base. The button row directly above it is `flex flex-wrap` with no cap and spans the full card, which is exactly the "narrow column under the format info" the requirement describes. The SVG entry is the first `<li>` and therefore the most visible instance.**
  - evidence: frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1656 — `<ul className="mt-2 grid max-w-prose gap-1 text-xs leading-5 text-ink-500">`; `prose: '65ch'` at frontend/node_modules/tailwindcss/stubs/config.full.js:671; no override in frontend/tailwind.config.ts or frontend/app/globals.css
  - change here: One class on frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1656 — drop `max-w-prose`, or widen it (e.g. `max-w-none`, or an explicit `max-w-[70ch]`/`max-w-4xl`). Note the `grid` on the same element is a single implicit column, so nothing else there constrains the row.
  - android twin: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/DwSketchTraceExportCard.kt:269 — the Android hint is a bare `Text(format.hint, …)` inside `Column(modifier = modifier.fillMaxWidth(), …)` at :231-233, so it already spans the card. Fixing the web brings it TOWARD Android, not away.
- **If the fix widens only the `<ul>`, it will become visibly wider than the four sibling explanatory paragraphs in the same block, all of which also carry `max-w-prose`. A consistent fix has to decide for the whole block, not one element.**
  - evidence: frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1663, :1670, :1697, :1720 — each is `<p className="mt-2 max-w-prose text-xs leading-5 text-ink-500">` (or `mt-1`, or `text-amber-800` at :1720)
  - change here: frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1656, 1663, 1670, 1697, 1720 — and the panel intro at :1751 if the whole panel's measure is being reconsidered.
  - android twin: NONE — Android has no measure cap on any of these; every one of its equivalents is a full-width `Text` in the fillMaxWidth Column.
- **The format list is not visible immediately after an image is processed. `{downloads}` renders only inside the advanced disclosure body, which is gated on `advancedMounted` / `advancedOpen`; the closed state instead shows the sentence "Inside: the part of the photograph to trace, the {ADVANCED_COUNT} settings that are not above, and the formats you can download a copy in." A designer must press "Show more options · {ADVANCED_COUNT} settings" first. If Requirement 8 expects the formats listed on the processed result, this gating is part of the gap.**
  - evidence: frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:2408 `{advancedMounted ? (` … :2444 `{downloads}`; the closed-state sentence is at :2384-2390; the toggle label at :2373-2375
  - change here: frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx — the `downloads` const would have to be rendered outside the `advancedMounted` block; the header comment at :1604-1609 argues for the current placement, so this is a deliberate decision to reverse rather than a bug.
  - android twin: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/FieldRenderer.kt:2001 — `DwSketchTraceExportCard(` is mounted directly, not behind a disclosure.
- **The web and Android present the descriptions differently: the web lists ALL FIVE sentences at once under the button row; Android shows only the SELECTED format's sentence under a FilterChip row. Not a defect in itself, but any restructuring of the web list should be a conscious choice about which shape to converge on.**
  - evidence: web: frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1657 maps every entry into an `<li>`. Android: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/DwSketchTraceExportCard.kt:250-264 renders chips, then :269-274 renders one `Text(format.hint)`.
  - change here: frontend/components/sketches/upload/SketchTraceField.tsx:1656-1662 (web list shape) — but see the e2e source-string assertions in risks before restructuring.
  - android twin: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/DwSketchTraceExportCard.kt:250-274

### Traps and risks

- THE FIVE FORMAT ENTRIES AND THEIR EXACT DESCRIPTION TEXT (frontend/components/sketches/upload/traceExport.ts:141-197). SVG — label "SVG", button "Download the trace (SVG)", hint: "The traced paths themselves. Scales to any size without ever going blocky, opens in Illustrator, Inkscape and CorelDRAW, and is what the “Line art” field is declared for." (:148-150). PNG — button "Download the rendered image (PNG)", hint: "The drawing rendered as a picture, transparent wherever the drawing is not, up to ${PNG_MAX_EDGE_PX}px on its long edge. Opens anywhere and drops straight into a letter or a slide, but it is pixels — enlarge it and it goes soft." (:164-165). PDF — button "Download a PDF to send on", hint: "Vector, and it opens on every machine you could mail it to without anybody installing anything. The one to attach to an email, or to hand to somebody who only needs to look at it." (:173-174). DXF — button "Download for a CAD or cutting machine (DXF)", hint: "The outlines as CAD geometry, for a laser cutter, a CNC router or a drafting package. It is DXF R12, which every controller reads: curves arrive as many short straight lines, and colour, fill and line thickness are not carried at all." (:184-185). EPS — button "Download for a print shop (EPS)", hint: "Vector PostScript, for a print shop or sign-cutting software that will not take an SVG. PostScript has no transparency, so anything part-see-through is flattened onto the background as the file is written." (:196-197).
- THE TEST READS THE PANEL AS A SOURCE STRING. frontend/e2e/sketch-export-formats-unit.spec.ts:172-188 does `readFileSync(PANEL_SOURCE)` and asserts the file CONTAINS the literal substrings `EXPORT_FORMATS.map((entry) => (`, `void downloadDerived(entry.id)`, `{entry.download}`, `{entry.hint}`, `running === \`download-${entry.id}\``, and `ATTACHABLE_FORMATS.map((entry) => (`. Restructuring the list JSX (e.g. destructuring `entry`, extracting a row component, or renaming the map parameter) breaks the suite even if the UI is correct. A pure class-string change on :1656 is safe.
- THE SAME SPEC FORBIDS HARD-CODING A LABEL: `expect(panel, \`${entry.id}'s label is hard-coded in the panel\`).not.toContain(entry.download)` (frontend/e2e/sketch-export-formats-unit.spec.ts:194-196). Do not inline any button text into the JSX while reworking the layout.
- THE DESCRIPTIONS CANNOT BE SHORTENED TO FIT A NARROW COLUMN. frontend/e2e/sketch-export-formats-unit.spec.ts:211-212 requires every `hint` to be longer than 60 characters and to end in a period. Trimming copy is not an available fix for the width problem.
- THE HINT STRINGS ARE A CROSS-CLIENT CONTRACT. android/.../DwSketchTraceExport.kt:419-477 carries all five hints verbatim from the web table, with a comment at DwSketchTraceExport.kt:432-435 explaining that the PNG row must interpolate `${DW_TRACE_PNG_MAX_EDGE_PX}px` with NO space to match `traceExport.ts:165`. Editing web hint text silently desynchronises the handset; the file header states the rule is that one format is described one way on both clients.
- frontend/e2e/sketch-trace-panel.spec.ts:944 pins the accessible name of the first button: `page.getByRole("button", { name: "Download the trace (SVG)" })`.
- THE `grid` ON THE `<ul>` IS NOT THE CULPRIT AND REMOVING IT WON'T HELP. `grid` with no `grid-cols-*` is a single implicit column; `gap-1` is the row gap between the five `<li>`s. There is no `col-span`, no flex child, no explicit width anywhere in this block. `max-w-prose` is the only width constraint in the entire ancestor chain up to the page.
- `max-w-prose` IS SIZE-RELATIVE. Because `ch` resolves against the element's own font-size and `text-xs` is on the same element, this cap is materially tighter than the identical class elsewhere in the codebase at `text-sm`/base size. If a wider measure is wanted, an explicit `max-w-[…]` in rem/px is more predictable than raising the `ch` count.

## 2. Android nav + screens (requirements 3, 19, 20)

**Summary.** The Android app has NO Jetpack Navigation at all — zero `androidx.navigation` references anywhere under `android/` (verified by repo-wide grep, 0 hits). Navigation is a hand-rolled two-layer state machine inside one 17,203-line file: a public `NavDestination` enum + `FIELD_NAV_ITEMS` list in `ui/AppNavigation.kt` is the menu model (labels, icons, groups, permission predicates, mirroring the web's `DynamicIslandNav.tsx`), and a private `sealed interface Screen` in `MainActivity.kt` is the actual routing target, joined by three exhaustive `when` tables (`openDestination`, `headerTitle`, `currentDestination`). The app shell is a right-anchored `ModalNavigationDrawer` plus a `FieldIslandNav` floating pill (the web dynamic island adapted to taps); there is no bottom bar and no `Scaffold`/`TopAppBar` at the app level. A first-run walkthrough ALREADY EXISTS and is fully wired: `NavDestination.WALKTHROUGH` is an ungated root entry that opens `WalkthroughDialog`, a 12-step Material3 `AlertDialog` gated by a SharedPreferences flag. Its content is now badly out of date — it carries 12 steps and says "the same ten steps in the same order on the web" while the web's `GUIDE_STEPS` holds 19, and it is a plain dialog with no motion, no deep links and no dashboard tile against the web's scroll-linked `/guide` page.

### What exists today

- There is NO Jetpack Navigation in the Android app. A repo-wide grep for `androidx.navigation` across all .kt and .kts files under android/ returns exactly 0 matches; `NavHost`, `rememberNavController` and `navigation-compose` likewise return nothing.
  - `android/app/build.gradle.kts (absence — verified by `grep -rn androidx.navigation android/ --include=*.kt --include=*.kts | wc -l` => 0)`
- Layer 1 of navigation is `enum class NavDestination` — 34 entries, the contract between the menu model and MainActivity's private routing table. Entries: DASHBOARD, WALKTHROUGH, RECORD_ARTISAN, RECORD_PRODUCT, DOCUMENT_PROCESS, RECORD_TOOL, TAKE_INTERVIEW, UPLOAD_MEDIA, ADD_CRAFT, RECORD_WORKSHOP, DESIGN_WORKSHOPS, SKETCHES_AND_PROTOTYPES, DESIGN_REVIEW, DESIGN_WORKSHOP_INSPECTIONS, CUSTOM_QUESTIONNAIRES, DESIGNER_PROFILE, DESIGNER_ROSTER, MY_ACTIVITY, TASKS, BROWSE_RECORDS, SCAN_CODE, MAP, VIEW_DATA, CONSOLIDATED_QUESTIONNAIRE, SHARE_DATA_ACCESS, ASSIGN_TOOLS, REVIEW, SETTINGS_HUB, MANAGE_USERS, ACCESS_ROSTER, SETTINGS, GIVE_FEEDBACK.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:427-638`
- `data class NavEntry(destination, label, icon, group, can, gate, adminSurface)` is the row model. `label` carries the contract comment "The EXACT web label. Both clients speak one language; never reword one side only." `can: (UserDto) -> Boolean` is the entitlement — when false the entry is NOT RENDERED (never rendered disabled).
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:639-656`
- `val FIELD_NAV_ITEMS: List<NavEntry>` is "The single source of truth for navigation, item-for-item and in the same order as the web's NAV_ITEMS. One list drives the whole menu, so a hidden entry cannot reappear somewhere else." Both the drawer and the island bar are projections of it.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:663`
- A WALKTHROUGH destination is ALREADY registered as a standalone root (group = null, so it renders loose above the four groups, mirroring the web pill). Exact text: `NavEntry(NavDestination.WALKTHROUGH, "Walkthrough", Icons.Filled.Explore, null, everyone, "none (static page)")`, preceded by the comment "Onboarding, deliberately ungated: the Walkthrough teaches the documentation process itself, so it has to reach the people who have not earned any capability yet".
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:665-668`
- `enum class NavGroup(val label: String)` = RECORD("Record"), BROWSE("Browse"), ADMIN("Admin"), ACCOUNT("Account") — in render order. `group = null` means a standalone root above the groups.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:399-405`
- Visibility filter: `isNavItemVisible(item, user, adminMode)` consults `item.can(user)` FIRST, then the admin-view toggle; `visibleNavItems(user, adminMode)` returns the filtered list in web order. `private val everyone: (UserDto) -> Boolean = { true }` is the ungated predicate.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:657, 801-809`
- Layer 2 is `private sealed interface Screen` — the real routing target, 33 variants. Objects/classes at: Dashboard:558, Create:559, Browse:569, Edit:570, MyActivity:572, ToolAssign:573, Feedback:574, Appearance:588, MyAiKeys:592, SpeechAndAi:604, DataBrowser:610, AdminHub:615, DesignWorkshops:643, DesignWorkshopStages:644, DesignWorkshopStage:654, DesignWorkshopReport:659, DesignWorkshopReportHistory:685, SketchesAndPrototypes:695, DesignReview:712, ScanCode:726, DesignWorkshopCodes:736, DesignWorkshopPhotos:746, DesignWorkshopViewers:763, DesignWorkshopInspectors:781, DesignWorkshopInspections:791, DesignWorkshopInspection:803, DesignWorkshopProvenance:827, DesignerProfile:838, DesignerRoster:841, AccessRoster:851, Questionnaires:867, QuestionnaireDetail:868, QuestionnaireAnswers:869.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:557-870`
- Route ARGUMENTS are constructor parameters on the `Screen` data classes — there is no string route, no Bundle, no type-safe nav args. Examples: `data class DesignWorkshopStage(val workshopId: String, val stageKey: String, val focus: DwStageFocus? = null)`, `data class DesignWorkshops(val startCreating: Boolean = false)`, `data class DesignerProfile(val userId: String? = null, val label: String? = null)`.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:643, 654-658, 838`
- `fun openDestination(destination: NavDestination)` is "The routing table behind the menu — the one place a NavDestination turns into a screen." It is EXHAUSTIVE WITH NO `else` BRANCH on purpose: "a catch-all here would let that happen again by letting a new destination render as a row that does nothing when tapped. Without one, adding to the enum breaks the build instead."
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:1787-1866`
- The WALKTHROUGH arm of the router sets a dialog flag rather than a screen: `NavDestination.WALKTHROUGH -> showWalkthrough = true`, commented "A dialog rather than a screen: the walkthrough overlays wherever the user already was."
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:1801-1802`
- `fun navigate(destination: NavDestination)` is the user-facing entry point: it closes the drawer, then routes WALKTHROUGH straight through (no unsaved-changes prompt, because "it draws OVER the page you are already on... prompting would offer to throw away a form that is not going anywhere") and every other destination through `attemptExit { openDestination(destination) }`.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:1868-1890`
- `val currentDestination: NavDestination?` is the inverse map — "Which menu row is the page you are on — the drawer's aria-current. The inverse of navigate, so the two are read side by side." It is null where no row honestly matches (Browse/Edit screens).
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:2058-2140`
- `val headerTitle: String?` is the third exhaustive table — the shared page heading above the scrolling content. Null means the screen draws its own heading.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:1979-2045`
- The app shell is a right-anchored `ModalNavigationDrawer` — wrapped in `CompositionLocalProvider(LocalLayoutDirection provides LayoutDirection.Rtl)` so the sheet slides in from the right for web parity, then flipped back to Ltr for the sheet and page content.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:2158-2205`
- The drawer content is `AppNavigationDrawerContent(user, adminMode, currentDestination, onNavigate, pendingAccessCount, pushingUpdate, onToggleAdminView, onPushUpdate, onLogout)` — a `ModalDrawerSheet` with a brand block, then `rootItems` (group == null) rendered as `NavRow`s, then each `NavGroup` with a `NavGroupHeading` and its rows, then admin-view toggle and master-admin update footer.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:1678-1809`
- `private fun NavRow(entry, selected, onNavigate, badge)` renders a Material3 `NavigationDrawerItem` with `label = { Text(entry.label, style = MaterialTheme.typography.labelLarge) }`, `icon = { Icon(entry.icon, contentDescription = null) }`, `onClick = { onNavigate(entry.destination) }`, `modifier = Modifier.padding(NavigationDrawerItemDefaults.ItemPadding)`.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:1838-1856`
- The second shell surface is `FieldIslandNav(roots, groups, onBrandClick, onOpenDrawer, modifier, adminMode, onToggleAdminView, currentLabel, scrollOffset, collapseAt, expandAt)` — "The web's dynamic island, on the phone." It sheds width in `IslandDensity` steps (FULL, MARK, MARK_COMPACT, ICON_GROUPS, ...) measured against real strings, and collapses to mark+wordmark+hamburger once the page scrolls past `ISLAND_COLLAPSE_SCROLL` (72.dp).
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:1150-1177, 1101-1102`
- The island's call site builds `roots` and `groups` from the SAME shared model: `val navItems = visibleNavItems(user, adminView)` then `roots = navItems.filter { it.group == null }.map { entry -> IslandEntry(entry.label, entry.icon) { navigate(entry.destination) } }` and `groups = NavGroup.entries.map { group -> ... }`. So a `group = null` NavEntry appears automatically as a root chip in the bar AND a root row in the drawer.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:2277, 2298-2316`
- There is NO bottom navigation bar and no app-level `Scaffold`/`TopAppBar`. The page chrome is a plain `Row` with a back `IconButton` (`Icons.AutoMirrored.Filled.ArrowBack`, contentDescription "Back") plus the `headerTitle` Text, drawn only when `screen !is Screen.Dashboard` or a title exists. It is a per-device flag, not a per-account server preference.

Title slot: `Text("Walkthrough · ${step + 1}/${steps.size}", color = Muted, fontSize = 11.sp)` over `Text(current.title, display = true, fontSize = 20.sp, ...)`. - The only DASHBOARD route into the walkthrough is a `TextButton(onClick = onWalkthrough) { Text("Open the walkthrough") }` inside the `if (!canCreateRecords)` explainer card — so a Researcher and above never sees a dashboard link to it at all. `onWalkthrough: () -> Unit = {}` is a DashboardScreen parameter (line 3814) wired at line 2411.

- Animation is plain Compose animation APIs — no shared motion file, no motion-token object, no Material transitions library. Only 13 files import `androidx.compose.animation` at all, and MainActivity.kt is NOT one of them. Imports used, by frequency: `core.tween` (12), `core.animateFloatAsState` (7), `core.snap` (3), `fadeIn`/`fadeOut` (2 each), `core.RepeatMode`/`rememberInfiniteTransition`/`LinearEasing`/`infiniteRepeatable`/`core.animateFloat`/`AnimatedVisibility` (2 each), and single uses of `togetherWith`, `slideIn/OutVertically`, `slideIn/OutHorizontally`, `shrinkHorizontally`, `scaleIn`, `expandHorizontally`, `EnterTransition`, `ExitTransition`, `core.animateDpAsState`, `core.Animatable`, `AnimatedContent`, `animateContentSize`.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:58-72 (and 12 other files)`
- The house motion vocabulary is `tween` with hand-tuned millisecond durations, asymmetric in/out. The island bar: `animateDpAsState(animationSpec = if (stillness) snap() else tween(180))`; wordmark `fadeIn(tween(180)) + expandHorizontally(tween(180), Alignment.Start)` in, `fadeOut(tween(110)) + shrinkHorizontally(tween(110), Alignment.Start)` out; admin chip `fadeIn(tween(160, delayMillis = 120)) + scaleIn(tween(200, delayMillis = 120), initialScale = 0.88f)` in, `fadeOut(tween(90))` out; collapsing strip `fadeIn(tween(180)) + slideInVertically(tween(180)) { -it / 4 }` in, `fadeOut(tween(120)) + slideOutVertically(tween(120)) { -it / 4 }` out.
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:1266-1268, 1401-1410, 1521-1535, 1573-1580`
- Reduced motion is a MANDATORY, universally-applied switch read from `LocalAppPreferences.current.reducedMotion` (a `staticCompositionLocalOf`). Every animated site collapses to `snap()` / `EnterTransition.None` / `ExitTransition.None` under it. AppNavigation names the local variable `stillness` and comments: "'Stops animations and transitions' is a promise the user made the app; a bar that slides and fades anyway would be the one place it is broken."
  - `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppNavigation.kt:1185; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/AppearanceScreen.kt:258, 277`
- Other reduced-motion honouring sites, all following the same `if (reduceMotion) snap() else tween(N)` shape: DwGalleryFloor (tween 300), DwPhotoMeasureField (tween 180), DwMediaCarousel (fadeIn/Out tween 90 when reduced, else slideInHorizontally tween 200 / slideOutHorizontally tween 160), DwRankableList (delay instead of withFrameNanos).

The entire design-token system is one 774-line file, `ui/Theme.kt`: `object FieldPalette` (raw literal tokens — a purple ramp Purple50..Purple950 converted from oklch, a marketing-only Gold ramp, and Light/Dark neutral ladders), `data class FieldTokens` (roles Material has no slot for), `LightFieldTokens`/`DarkFieldTokens`, `val LocalFieldTokens = staticCompositionLocalOf { LightFieldTokens }` and the accessor `val MaterialTheme.field: FieldTokens`.

### Traps and risks

- THERE IS ALREADY A WALKTHROUGH. Do not build a second one. `NavDestination.WALKTHROUGH` (AppNavigation.kt:429), its nav entry (AppNavigation.kt:668), its router arm (MainActivity.kt:1801-1802), its first-run trigger (MainActivity.kt:1661-1663), its seen-flag (MainActivity.kt:10348-10366), its 12 steps (MainActivity.kt:10384-10487) and its dialog (MainActivity.kt:10496) all ship today. Adding a parallel 'guide' destination would be exactly the two-answers-to-one-question failure the codebase repeatedly refuses in writing — see the deleted `Screen.Settings` note at MainActivity.kt:575-586 ('a third answer to "where do settings live" that only a person can delete').
- `openDestination` is EXHAUSTIVE WITH NO `else` BRANCH BY DESIGN (MainActivity.kt:1793-1796). Adding a NavDestination enum member without adding its router arm is a COMPILE ERROR, which is intentional. Conversely, `headerTitle` (1979) and `currentDestination` (2058) are also exhaustive `when`s over `Screen` — adding a new `Screen` variant means three tables must be updated, plus `goBack()` (1892).
- THE WALKTHROUGH IS DELIBERATELY NOT A `Screen`. It is a dialog flag so it overlays the current page, and `navigate()` special-cases it to SKIP the unsaved-changes guard (MainActivity.kt:1878-1884: 'there is nothing to save or discard, and prompting would offer to throw away a form that is not going anywhere'). Converting it to a full `Screen` destination would silently reintroduce that prompt and would break the 'reopen from anywhere without losing my form' property.
- `currentDestination` has a live scar: a comment placed INSIDE a comma-separated `when` arm list once handed SKETCHES_AND_PROTOTYPES to five unrelated screens ('which is what it did until 2026-08-26, five stage screens lighting a row that does not open them' — MainActivity.kt:2079-2085). A comment does not terminate a `when` branch. Place comments BELOW a comma-list, never between its members.
- LABELS ARE PINNED TO THE WEB, BYTE FOR BYTE. `NavEntry.label` carries the contract 'The EXACT web label. Both clients speak one language; never reword one side only' (AppNavigation.kt:641). The existing label is "Walkthrough" on both clients (AppNavigation.kt:668 and frontend/components/DynamicIslandNav.tsx:175 `{ href: "/guide", label: "Walkthrough", icon: Compass, group: null, can: everyone, gate: "none (static page)" }`). Do not rename it to "Guide", "Tour" or "Getting started" on one side.
- `DashboardTileParityTest` holds the Android and web dashboard grids to each other string for string, so a walkthrough TILE cannot be added to one client without the other in the same change (the SCAN_CODE destination's KDoc at MainActivity.kt / AppNavigation.kt states this explicitly as the reason a destination was added rather than a panel moved).
- REDUCED MOTION IS NON-NEGOTIABLE HERE. Every animated surface in the app reads `LocalAppPreferences.current.reducedMotion` and collapses to `snap()`/`EnterTransition.None`. AppNavigation.kt:1183-1185 states the rule: "'Stops animations and transitions' is a promise the user made the app; a bar that slides and fades anyway would be the one place it is broken." An animated walkthrough that ignores it breaks a documented invariant.
- THE ISLAND BAR IS WIDTH-MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. `rememberIslandWidths` (AppNavigation.kt:989) measures the REAL label strings to pick an `IslandDensity` tier. A walkthrough entry is already a `group == null` ROOT chip, and roots are the LAST thing the bar gives up (AppNavigation.kt:828-834). Renaming the entry to something longer, or adding a second root, changes the density thresholds for every phone — re-check against a 360dp width.
- `scrollOffset` must be passed to FieldIslandNav as a LAMBDA, never a value: 'handing over the number reads the scroll state in the CALLER's composition and recomposes the caller on every frame of every scroll, which on this app means the whole screen' (AppNavigation.kt:1164-1170).
- `walkthroughSteps` and `WalkStep` are `private` in MainActivity.kt (10369, 10384) and therefore untestable. The codebase's own precedent for fixing this is `internal fun navBadge` (AppNavigation.kt:1831), hoisted out of the drawer specifically so `AppNavigationBadgeTest` could assert it — follow that, do not add a test-only duplicate list.
- MainActivity.kt is 17,203 lines and holds LoginScreen, HomeScreen, DashboardScreen, the whole routing table, the walkthrough and ~90 other private composables. Edits there are high-collision; per the user's stored memory, scripted Python `write_text` edits on this repo silently flip LF to CRLF, which breaks specs that slice source by a literal newline-brace. Edit with Edit/sed, not with a Python rewrite.

---

## 3. Landing page (frontend/app/page.tsx + frontend/components/hero/*) — section-by-section feature inventory, logos, and institutional branding (Requirements 1, 15, 16)

**Summary.** The public landing page is one server component (`frontend/app/page.tsx`, 28 lines) whose only job is to fetch the corpus census and render a single client island, `HeroLanding`. `HeroLanding` renders 13 bands in order: a dark hero, `#records`, `#how-it-works`, `#workshop`, `#designer`, `#report`, a walkthrough callout, the printing-bed/India-map band, `#access`, a team band, `#faq`, a closing CTA, and a footer. Every claim on the page is text and lucide-react icons — there is NOT ONE raster or file-based image anywhere: `frontend/public/` contains only `boundaries/` (three text/JSON files) and `git ls-files` returns zero `.svg/.png/.jpg/.webp/.ico` assets in the whole repo. The ONLY brand mark on the page is `WorkshopLogo`, a 30-line inline-SVG component (an 8-point terracotta star, near-black disc, cream tile) rendered twice — masthead and footer — and duplicated as the favicon at `frontend/app/icon.svg` and as the Android launcher at `android/app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_launcher.xml`. There is NO IIT Kharagpur logo, NO DC Handicrafts / Ministry of Textiles logo, NO Centre-of-Excellence link, and in fact NO external `href` of any kind on the landing page — every link is an internal Next `<Link>` or an on-page `#anchor`. Separately, three things `PrintingBed` documents at length (a gold Bagru impression, three open registration marks, and a `<figcaption>` naming the places) plus the CC-BY map attribution are NOT rendered by the current code, though the CSS and data for them still ship.

### What exists today

- The landing route is a server component whose only fetch is the corpus census; it renders exactly one component, `<HeroLanding census={census} />`. Page metadata title (absolute): "Design Prototype Workshop — The workshop ends. The report is already written." and description: "A capture-to-report tool for craft and textile designers: every stage of a design & prototype development workshop recorded at Basic, Standard and Advanced tiers, with photographs, costings and structured interviews — then rendered as a .docx or PDF report. Captured offline, and generated on the phone itself."

- **One tier count on the page is hand-typed rather than derived, against the file-level guarantee the rest of the page keeps. `HeroFAQ` says "eight-tier ladder" as a literal string in two answers, while the hero badge and the `#access` heading both interpolate `TIER_COUNT_WORD` from `ROLES_BY_RANK.length`. A ninth role would move the heading and the badge and leave the FAQ saying eight.**
  - evidence: frontend/components/hero/HeroFAQ.tsx:33 ("you join the eight-tier ladder") and :79 ("governed by the eight-tier role ladder"), versus frontend/components/hero/AccessLadder.tsx:145 exporting the derived `TIER_COUNT_WORD` and HeroLanding.tsx:63 consuming it
  - change here: frontend/components/hero/HeroFAQ.tsx:33 and :79 — import `TIER_COUNT_WORD` from `@/components/hero/AccessLadder` the way HeroLanding.tsx:25 already does
  - android twin: NONE

### Traps and risks

- THE LOGO IS A THREE-WAY HAND-COPY. WorkshopLogo.tsx:6-10 states the path data is "a hand-transcription of the Android drawable, so the two must be edited together". There is in fact a THIRD copy at frontend/app/icon.svg. Any change to the mark must land in all three: frontend/components/WorkshopLogo.tsx:23-27, frontend/app/icon.svg:2-4, android/app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_launcher.xml:7-9. Nothing in the build enforces this.
- THE LOGO'S COLOURS ARE EXEMPT FROM THEMING. WorkshopLogo.tsx:4-5: "Its colors are brand-native and are never re-themed; on dark surfaces keep the cream tile." Do not swap #FAF9F5 / #CC785C / #181715 for tokens, and do not pass `tile={false}` on a dark band.
- GOLD IS BUDGETED. HeroLanding.tsx:127 — "Gold is permitted here (and on auth) and nowhere else", and DesignWorkshopSection.tsx:50-53 says the budget is already spent on the hero, the printing bed and the closing band; "a fourth gold surface would make the bed stop being the one signature moment". A new logo strip must not introduce gold.
- THREE COUNTS ARE DERIVED, NOT TYPED, AND THE FILES ARGUE AT LENGTH ABOUT WHY. `TIER_COUNT_WORD` (AccessLadder.tsx:137-145), `STAGE_COUNT`/`STAGE_COUNT_WORD` (workshopArc.ts:105-131), and the renderer column count (`lg:grid-cols-6` in ReportEngine.tsx:246, which ReportEngine.tsx:224 says "is the list's length and not a number of its own"). Do not replace any of them with a literal.
- BRAND PURPLE NEVER INVERTS. DesignWorkshopSection.tsx:129-133 documents this as a shipped bug: a `bg-purple-50` panel is near-white in BOTH themes, and `text-ink-900` on it is near-white in dark — white-on-white. The only text colours safe on a purple tint are the non-inverting purple rungs (`text-purple-700`). A logo strip on a tinted panel will hit exactly this.
- EVERY HERO SECTION IS `whileInView` WITH `opacity: 0` IN THE SERVER HTML. PrintingBed.tsx:333-348 notes that with scripting off the content stays at opacity 0, and that only that one band ships a `<noscript>` fix. Adding a logo band means adding another section invisible without JS unless the same `<noscript>` trick is repeated.
- REDUCED MOTION MUST CHANGE DURATIONS, NEVER THE `initial` STATE. useHeroMotion.ts:19-21: "The server always renders as if motion were allowed, so an initial state that depended on this hook would hydrate to different inline styles." Both switches (OS preference AND the in-app `data-reduced-motion` attribute) are OR-ed by `useHeroReducedMotion`.
- THE ROUTE IS STATICALLY PRERENDERED AND HAS NO AUTH CONTEXT. app/page.tsx:12-15 — the census fetch uses `revalidate: 300` specifically to keep the page static and never throws. useHeroMotion.ts:16-18 notes the landing page "cannot read ThemeProvider — it has no auth and no provider context". Two client-bundle imports are already flagged as costly on this page: `@/lib/permissions` (~20 KB, measured, AccessLadder.tsx:55-66) and `@/lib/reportTheme` (~12 KB, explicitly NOT measured, ReportEngine.tsx:88-95).
- THE PAGE HAS A DOCUMENTED NO-FABRICATION RULE. HeroLanding.tsx:289-311 records that a fabricated interview quote once shipped here and was removed because "on a repository whose entire product is citable provenance, fabricating a primary source on the marketing page is the most expensive thing it could possibly do". PrintingBed.tsx:49-55 applies the same rule to graphics — four identical dots "would be a lie told in graphics". Adding an institutional logo asserts an affiliation; the same standard applies.
- THE MASTHEAD LOGO IS NOT A LINK ON THE LANDING PAGE (HeroLanding.tsx:193-196 is a bare `<div>`), unlike every other surface where it is wrapped in `<Link href="/">` (login page:294) or `<Link href="/dashboard">` (DynamicIslandNav.tsx:680). Adding partner logos beside it should not silently make the house mark clickable.
- THE MAP OUTLINE IS NOT SWAPPABLE. PrintingBed.tsx:45-47: "the boundary follows the official Government of India depiction, it was verified by measurement rather than by trusting a README, and it must not be swapped for a convenient npm dataset." Its licence credit is currently unrendered (see gaps).

---

## 4. Web walkthrough — /guide, its 19 steps, its motion/layout mechanics, its coverage gaps and its entry points (requirements 2, 3, 19)

**Summary.** The web walkthrough is one scrolling page at `frontend/app/(protected)/guide/page.tsx`: a `PageHeader`, a dark hero band, a scroll-linked "journey" of 19 expandable step cards threaded onto a vertical spine with a sticky progress rail beside it, and a three-panel outro. All content lives in one array, `GUIDE_STEPS` in `frontend/components/guide/steps.ts` (771 lines) — 10 repository-record steps followed by 9 design-&-prototype-workshop steps — and each step carries an id, an Android-parity `label`, a dashboard-verb `action`, a lucide icon, an `href` to the real screen, a one-sentence `summary`, a `why` paragraph, a `fields[]` chip list mirroring the real form labels, and a `watch[]` list of cautions. Motion is framer-motion everywhere except one thing: the hero headline's per-word overlapping entrance, which is a dynamically-imported GSAP timeline (`useGsapHeadline.ts`). Every animation, including smooth scrolling, is gated on `useAppReducedMotion()` — the OR of the OS `prefers-reduced-motion` query and the app's own Settings toggle. The guide is entered from the dynamic-island nav ("Walkthrough"), from four "Open the walkthrough" lock-panel buttons, from the dashboard's volunteer notice, and from four places on the public landing page including `WalkthroughCallout`; the Android twin (`WalkthroughDialog` in `MainActivity.kt`) is a 12-card AlertDialog that still carries only the ten record steps and none of the nine-step workshop arc.

### What exists today

- The page is four components in a single scroll, with no tabs or pagination: `<PageHeader title="Walkthrough" description={...} icon={<Compass className="h-5 w-5"/>} />`, then `<GuideHero stepCount={GUIDE_STEPS.length} onStart={() => scrollToStep(GUIDE_STEPS[0].id, reduce)} />`, then `<GuideJourney />`, then `<GuideOutro />`.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/guide/page.tsx:36-53`
- The header description is built by string concatenation and derives its count: "How a craft gets documented and how a design & prototype workshop is run, from your own designer profile to the report a ministry officer receives. ${GUIDE_STEPS.length} steps, in the order you do them." — i.e. it renders as "19 steps, in the order you do them." The comment above records that a hardcoded "Ten steps" had gone stale twice.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/guide/page.tsx:25-45`
- `GUIDE_STEPS` holds exactly 19 steps in two arcs — ten record steps (`workshop`, `craft`, `artisan`, `product`, `process`, `tool`, `questionnaire`, `media`, `review`, `view-data`) then nine workshop steps (`designer-profile`, `design-workshop`, `design-workshop-codes`, `design-workshop-stages`, `design-workshop-sketches`, `design-review`, `design-workshop-readiness`, `design-workshop-report`, `design-workshop-history`).
  - `frontend/components/guide/steps.ts:127-771`
- The `GuideStep` type is: `id` (stable anchor, the rail scrolls to `#${id}` and the hash survives reload), `label` (Android-parity feature name — "Never invent a new one"), `action` (the dashboard tile's verb), `icon` (LucideIcon), `href` (the real screen), `summary` (one sentence), `why` (why the dataset needs it), `fields` (the real form labels in screen order, required ones marked), `watch` (field-tested cautions).
  - `frontend/components/guide/steps.ts:107-125`

Products, tools and interviews all carry a linked workshop, and the Data Browser opens on \"By workshop\", which files the whole repository under the workshop it was recorded in. On a create form the most recent workshop you have access to is preselected, so getting this right once saves you picking it on every screen afterwards." 9 fields (Workshop title (required) … Location (GPS fix or map pin)), 2 watch bullets.

Each `summary` names the screen so the reader can find it. `design-workshop` is a sixth `/design-workshops` href, but there the list IS the screen.
  - `frontend/components/guide/steps.ts:400-422`
- HERO LAYOUT: a `surface-dark` band, `px-6 py-10 sm:px-10 sm:py-12`, `relative isolate overflow-hidden`. Contents top to bottom: eyebrow "Walkthrough" (uppercase, tracking .14em, purple-300); the H2 "Document a craft, end to end."; a paragraph — "This is the whole process, in the order you actually perform it: the repository records first, then the 22-stage design & prototype workshop they feed and the report that comes out of it. Each step below names the screen it lives on, the fields it asks for, and the mistakes that cost people a second trip to the field."; three fact tiles in `sm:grid-cols-3`; then the CTA button.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideHero.tsx:80-128`
- The three hero facts, in order, with icons: `ShieldCheck` "Access is by invitation — an admin admits your address first"; `ListOrdered` `${stepCount} steps, in the order you do them in the field` (renders "19 steps…"); `Compass` "Every record is scoped to a workshop". The comment records that the first fact replaced a review fact deliberately, because the guide's readers include people whose sign-in was refused by the platform allow-list.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideHero.tsx:38-56`
- The hero CTA is a `motion.button` labelled "Start at step 1" with a trailing `ArrowDown`, `bg-purple-700 … shadow-cta hover:bg-purple-800`, `whileHover {y:-2}`, `whileTap {scale:0.97}`. It calls `scrollToStep(GUIDE_STEPS[0].id, reduce)` — i.e. it scrolls to the `workshop` card, smoothly unless reduced motion is on.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideHero.tsx:116-128`
- The hero's only ambient motion is a pointer-tracked purple wash: two motion values (default 50/50, i.e. dead centre) spring-smoothed at `{stiffness:110, damping:24, mass:0.6}` and composed by `useTransform` into `radial-gradient(30rem 26rem at ${x}% ${y}%, oklch(0.648 0.19 305 / 0.42), transparent 64%)` on an `absolute inset-0 -z-10` layer. With no pointer, or under reduced motion (the `onPointerMove`/`onPointerLeave` handlers become `undefined`), it renders as a static centred gradient.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideHero.tsx:25-36`
- GSAP owns exactly one element on the page: the hero H2. `useGsapHeadline` splits the heading's text into per-word `inline-block` spans once (guarded by `node.dataset.split`, spaces kept OUTSIDE the spans so line-breaking and copy behave), then `import("gsap")` dynamically (~70 KB kept out of every protected page) and builds a timeline with `defaults: { ease: "power3.out", duration: 0.62 }`, tweening each word `fromTo({yPercent:108, opacity:0, rotate:1.5}, {yPercent:0, opacity:1, rotate:0})` at position `"<0.28"` — each word starts 0.28s after the previous STARTED, while the previous is still 0.34s from finishing. That overlap is the stated reason GSAP is used at all; framer's `staggerChildren` cannot express it.
  - `frontend/components/guide/useGsapHeadline.ts:28-86`
- Under reduced motion `useGsapHeadline` builds no timeline at all and clears the spans' inline `transform`/`opacity` — the words are left exactly as rendered, not animated quickly. Cleanup kills the timeline and sets a `cancelled` flag so a late dynamic import does nothing.
  - `frontend/components/guide/useGsapHeadline.ts:53-86`
- JOURNEY LAYOUT: `<section className="mt-10 grid gap-8 lg:grid-cols-[260px_minmax(0,1fr)]" aria-label="The documentation process, step by step">` — a fixed 260px rail column on the left at `lg` and up, the step list on the right. Below `lg` the rail is not rendered (`hidden lg:block`) and the spine alone carries the reader.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideJourney.tsx:69-70`
- The step list is an `<ol className="relative grid gap-4 [--guide-rail:2rem] sm:[--guide-rail:3rem]">`. `--guide-rail` is the single owner of the spine's horizontal axis: the spine is a `w-[var(--guide-rail)]` flex box at `left-0` with `justify-center`, and each card is `grid-cols-[var(--guide-rail,2rem)_minmax(0,1fr)] gap-x-4` so its number bubble is a grid item in the same column. Track, fill, travelling node and every numbered bubble therefore share one centre line by construction.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideJourney.tsx:73-91`
- SCROLL LINKING: `const { scrollYProgress } = useScroll({ target: listRef, offset: ["start 65%", "end 65%"] })` — progress starts when the list's top passes 65% down the viewport and completes when the list's bottom reaches the same line, so the spine reads 100% while the last step is still on screen. It is smoothed by `useSpring(scrollYProgress, { stiffness: 140, damping: 30, mass: 0.4 })`; under reduced motion the RAW value is used instead (fill tracks the scrollbar with no inertia).
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideJourney.tsx:43-50`
- One progress value feeds three consumers, none of which re-render React: the spine fill's `scaleY`, the travelling node's `top` (`useTransform(progress, [0,1], ["0%","100%"])`), and the rail's progress ring. The spine itself is a 2px column — `absolute inset-y-6 left-0`, a static track `bg-line-200`, a `motion.span` fill with `style={{scaleY: progress}} origin-top bg-purple-700`, and (only when motion is allowed) a 10px dot `h-2.5 w-2.5 rounded-full bg-purple-700 ring-4 ring-purple-100` positioned by margins (`-left-1 -mt-[5px]`), never by transforms.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideJourney.tsx:82-107`
- CARD EXPANSION: exactly one card is open at a time — `useState<string|null>(GUIDE_STEPS[0].id)`, so step 1 is open on arrival "so the shape of a step is obvious without the reader having to discover that the cards expand". Clicking the open card's header collapses it to none; clicking another card's header swaps. The rail's `jump(id)` sets the id open AND scrolls to it.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideJourney.tsx:39-66`
- DEEP LINKS: on mount the journey reads `window.location.hash`; if it matches a step id it expands that step and, after one `requestAnimationFrame` (so the expanded card has its final height), calls `scrollToStep(hash, true)` — the `true` forces an INSTANT jump regardless of the motion preference. So `/guide#questionnaire` opens and lands on that step.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideJourney.tsx:53-61`
- CARD ANATOMY: a `motion.li` with `id={step.id}`, `layout={reduce ? false : "position"}`, `scroll-mt-28` (clearance for the floating header pill), two grid columns. Column 1 is the numbered bubble — `h-8 w-8 rounded-full grad-brand font-display text-xs font-bold text-white shadow-md`, `mt-6`, `justify-self-center`, `z-10`, showing `{index + 1}` and animating `scale` to 1.12 while its card is expanded. Column 2 is the card: `rounded-lg border border-line-200 bg-card shadow-sm`, deliberately NOT `overflow-hidden` so the global focus outline is not clipped.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideStepCard.tsx:50-88`
- CARD REVEAL: the card body uses `variants={staggerParent(reduce)} initial="hidden" whileInView="show" viewport={{ once: true, amount: 0.25 }}` — a one-time reveal when 25% of the card is visible, `staggerChildren: 0.06` after `delayChildren: 0.05`, each child using `riseItem(reduce, 10)` = opacity 0→1 with y 10→0 over 0.5s on ease `[0.16, 1, 0.3, 1]`. The staggered order is icon tile → (label + action pill) → summary → chevron, i.e. "the reading order, enforced in time".
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideStepCard.tsx:82-133`
- The collapsed card header is a full-width button, `flex w-full items-start gap-4 p-5 text-left`: a `h-10 w-10 rounded-md bg-purple-800` tile holding the step's white `h-5 w-5` lucide icon; then the label in `font-display text-lg font-bold` beside an action pill (`rounded-full bg-purple-50 px-2.5 py-0.5 text-xs font-medium text-purple-800`); then the summary in `mt-2 text-sm leading-6`; then a `h-7 w-7` bordered circle holding a `ChevronDown` that rotates 180° when expanded. `whileHover {y:-2}`, `whileTap {scale:0.995}`, `aria-expanded`, and `aria-controls` set only while the panel is mounted.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideStepCard.tsx:89-134`
- The detail panel is inside `AnimatePresence initial={false}`: `initial/exit {height:0, opacity:0}` → `animate {height:"auto", opacity:1}`, with the height on `layoutSpring` and opacity over 0.18s (both `duration: 0` under reduced motion). It clips itself (`overflow-hidden rounded-b-lg`) and its body is `border-t border-line-200 bg-surface-50 p-5` with `grid gap-5`.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideStepCard.tsx:136-155`
- The expanded panel always renders four blocks in this fixed order: (1) `Lightbulb` + heading "Why this step exists" over `step.why` as a paragraph; (2) `ListChecks` + heading "What the screen asks for" over `step.fields` as flex-wrapped pill chips (`rounded-full border border-line-200 bg-card px-3 py-1 text-xs`), each entering with `slideItem(reduce, 8)` (x −8→0 over 0.4s) at 0.025s stagger; (3) `TriangleAlert` + heading "Watch out for" over `step.watch` as a bulleted list with 1×1 purple dots; (4) a link styled `field-button h-9 min-h-0 px-3.5 text-xs` reading `Open {step.label}` with a trailing `ArrowUpRight`, pointing at `step.href`.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideStepCard.tsx:156-202`
- ACTIVE-STEP TRACKING: each card fires `onViewportEnter={onEnterView}` with `viewport={{ margin: "-45% 0px -45% 0px", amount: "some" }}` — i.e. when the card occupies the middle 10% band of the viewport. That sets `activeIndex` in the journey, which is the only thing the rail's readout and highlight consume. Reveal and tracking use two different elements because they need different thresholds.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideStepCard.tsx:54-56`
- RAIL: `<aside className="hidden lg:block">` containing `<div className="sticky top-28">` and a `panel p-5`. Top row is a progress ring beside a text readout. The ring is an 80×80 SVG rotated −90°, radius 34, circumference 2π·34; a track circle `stroke-line-200 strokeWidth 6` and a `motion.circle` in `oklch(0.47 0.198 305)` with `strokeLinecap="round"`, `strokeDasharray = C` and `strokeDashoffset` transformed from progress `[0,1] → [C, 0]`. The centre shows an integer percentage, updated through `useMotionValueEvent` with hard rounding so React re-renders at most 100 times for the whole page.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideRail.tsx:56-82`
- The rail's readout is three lines swapped through `AnimatePresence mode="wait"`: `Step {activeIndex + 1} of {steps.length}` (xs, uppercase, purple-700), the active step's `label` (`font-display text-lg font-bold`), and the active step's `action` (xs, ink-500). `swapVariants` moves the outgoing block out to y −8 over 0.16s on easeIn before the incoming one arrives from y 8 over 0.28s on the EASE_OUT curve.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideRail.tsx:87-103`
- The rail's step list is a `<nav aria-label="Walkthrough steps">` wrapping `<ol className="grid max-h-[calc(100vh-19rem)] min-h-[12rem] gap-0.5 overflow-y-auto pr-1">` — it scrolls INSIDE itself. The comment records why: the panel is `sticky top-28`, so at ten steps (~470px) the question never arose, but at nineteen (~770px) it overflowed a 1366×768 laptop and silently swallowed the last five entries, the whole designer arc among them.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideRail.tsx:106-124`
- Each rail row is a `motion.button` calling `onJump(step.id)`, `whileHover {x:3}`, `whileTap {scale:0.98}`. It shows a `h-5 w-5` round number badge — filled `bg-purple-700 text-white` when the row is active OR PAST, otherwise `bg-surface-50 text-ink-500 ring-1 ring-line-200` — and a truncated label. The active row gets `bg-purple-50 font-semibold text-purple-800`, `aria-current="step"`, and a 3px left marker `motion.span` with `layoutId="guide-rail-marker"` that slides between rows on `layoutSpring`.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideRail.tsx:125-161`
- The rail is deliberately silent to assistive technology — no `aria-live` region — on the stated reasoning that the readout changes every time a card crosses the viewport band, the steps are already a numbered `<ol>` with per-card headings, and `aria-current="step"` carries position. "The rail is a scroll indicator, not an event."
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideRail.tsx:24-28`
- The shared motion vocabulary is six factories in `guideMotion.ts`, every one taking the `reduce` flag and collapsing to zero duration / zero displacement: `EASE_OUT = [0.16, 1, 0.3, 1]`; `springy` = `{type:"spring", stiffness:380, damping:30, mass:0.7}` (every press/hover response); `layoutSpring` = `{stiffness:260, damping:32, mass:0.9}` (cards growing); `staggerParent(reduce, stagger=0.06)` with `delayChildren: 0.05`; `riseItem(reduce, distance=14)` at 0.5s; `slideItem(reduce, distance=12)` at 0.4s. Gating happens at the source "so a new animation in this folder cannot accidentally ship without honouring the preference".
  - `frontend/components/guide/guideMotion.ts:17-64`
- `scrollToStep(id, reduce)` is `document.getElementById(id)?.scrollIntoView({ behavior: reduce ? "auto" : "smooth", block: "start" })` — smooth scrolling is treated as motion and gated too.
  - `frontend/components/guide/guideMotion.ts:71-74`
- `useAppReducedMotion()` returns `useReducedMotion() ?? false || preferences.reducedMotion` — the OS `prefers-reduced-motion` media query OR'ed with the app's own Settings toggle (which stamps `data-reduced-motion="true"` on `<html>` via ThemeProvider). framer-motion's own hook sees only the OS half, so this hook is the required one everywhere in the guide. Server and first client render both read false, and the mount effect corrects it a tick later.
  - `frontend/components/guide/useAppReducedMotion.ts:31-34`
- Under reduced motion the concrete differences are: no GSAP headline timeline; no hero pointer glow; no travelling spine node (`{reduce ? null : <motion.span …>}`); the spine fill uses the raw unsprung scroll value; `layout` animation off on the list items; all rise/slide/stagger/swap collapse to instant; hover/tap transforms removed (`whileHover={reduce ? undefined : …}`); and "Start at step 1" / rail jumps become instant scrolls.
  - `frontend/components/guide/GuideJourney.tsx:30-33`

It is the same order on the web and in the Android app, and the screens carry the same names in both." then all 19 labels as numbered chips separated by "→". Run this list while the artisan is still in front of you." then an 8-item checklist in `sm:grid-cols-2` with `CheckCircle2` marks. (3) "Where to go next" + six link cards in `sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3`.

The stated test for inclusion is whether the item can still be fixed from a desk.

View Data" and its intro card is literally titled "Ten steps, in this order" while the web guide's own header says "19 steps, in the order you do them". This is the exact failure the web `steps.ts` header describes fixing on the web side in Aug 2026 — a designer opening the walkthrough looking for the deliverable finds ten ways to file a record and no path to the report.**

### Traps and risks

- `steps.ts` and `docs/WALKTHROUGH.md` are declared TWO RENDERINGS OF ONE THING and must move in the same commit — adding, removing or reordering a step without updating the doc fails `frontend/e2e/guide-walkthrough-unit.spec.ts:114-141`, and reordering the workshop arc without updating the `WORKSHOP_ARC` constant at :51-61 fails :63-71. That constant is a THIRD copy of the order and its own comment says update it in the same edit, never afterwards.
- No step may ever say a reference picker re-reads a live record. Choosing a record COPIES its values onto the stage entry and the report prints the frozen copy (authority: `REFERENCE_HYDRATION` in backend/app/services/stage_schema.py). The spec at frontend/e2e/guide-walkthrough-unit.spec.ts:91-112 regex-fails on /re-?reads? the (live )?record|shows the linked record|reads the linked record|stays in sync with the record/i across the whole arc, and asserts the stages step positively contains "COPIES its values" and "report prints that copy".
- Every `label` must be the Android-parity feature name and every `fields[]` entry must be the real form label in screen order, copied off the named component — not paraphrased. steps.ts:97-105 records that exactly three cards are allowed to break this (`design-workshop-codes`, `design-workshop-readiness` render no labelled field; `design-workshop-stages` cannot enumerate a server-published registry) and warns not to let a seventh exception join quietly.
- Do not centre anything on the spine with a Tailwind `-translate-x-1/2`. framer-motion writes its own inline `transform` for `scaleY` and for the bubbles' `scale`, which silently overrides a class transform — that is how the fill ended up 1px off the track and every numbered bubble 16px right of it. Centring is done by flex/`justify-self-center` inside a `--guide-rail`-wide box, and the travelling node by negative margins (GuideJourney.tsx:82-107, GuideStepCard.tsx:65-71).
- `--guide-rail` (2rem, sm:3rem) is the single owner of the horizontal axis, set once on the `<ol>` in GuideJourney and consumed by both the spine box and every card's first grid column. An Android port must reproduce the same single-source rule or the bubbles and the spine will drift; the previous three-way agreement (`absolute left-4 sm:left-6` twice plus `pl-12 sm:pl-16`) had already broken once.
- Never call framer-motion's `useReducedMotion()` directly in this folder — it can only see the OS media query. The app's second switch is the Settings toggle that stamps `data-reduced-motion="true"` on `<html>`, and only `useAppReducedMotion()` ORs both. Smooth scrolling counts as motion and is gated too.
- GSAP must stay dynamically imported and must own exactly one thing (the hero headline). Two animation systems on one property is the named trap; the ~70 KB is deliberately kept out of every protected page's bundle (useGsapHeadline.ts:65-67).
- The card must not become `overflow-hidden`: the global focus ring is an `outline` drawn outside the border box at `outline-offset: 2px`, and clipping the card erases it on three sides of every step's primary control. Only the collapsible panel clips, and it clips itself (GuideStepCard.tsx:26-30, :148).
- `aria-controls` is set only while the detail panel is mounted, because AnimatePresence removes the node on collapse and an aria-controls pointing at a missing id is worse than none (GuideStepCard.tsx:93-96). The rail is deliberately NOT an aria-live region (GuideRail.tsx:24-28) — adding one would announce ten-plus times on the way down the page.
- Four of the nine workshop steps land a non-designer on a "Designer access required" lock panel (`canRunDesignWorkshops` in lib/permissions.ts). This is deliberate and unfixable from steps.ts, so those four cards each carry the SAME sentence in `watch`, one wording repeated so it reads as a rule (steps.ts:67-76, and the sentence at :505, :541, :646, :673). Do not reword one of the four in isolation.
- Several `watch` bullets carry corrections that were previously written wrong from a neighbour's copy rather than from the code — the CV annexure claim (steps.ts:473-484), the "list of twenty-five" prototypes (steps.ts:551-557), the sketch-rectify "does not do" claim (steps.ts:50-65), the offline questionnaire annexure (steps.ts:730-738), and "the ones a submit is refused without" (steps.ts:584-591). Any Android port must copy the CURRENT wording, not an older doc, and the general rule written there applies: absence proved inside one directory is not absence from the product.

---

## 5. Recent feature changes — the definitive inventory of what shipped in the last ~15 commits (HEAD 7c60e81 back to 93c70b0, plus the four highly user-visible commits just beyond it), and what the landing page / walkthrough / in-app guide do and do not already say about it

**Summary.** The last 20 commits contain exactly four feature waves and thirteen release/CI/docs-regeneration commits. The four waves are 3def0f6 (2026-08-28, "Close the parity gaps on sketches, workshops and questionnaires, and put a microphone on every record page" — 104 files, +16,894), 7b78836 ("Replace the JS tracer with the native engine, give the inspector a client, and gate the photographs" — 501 files, +137,406), a9adb47 ("Name the designer a workshop is opened for"), and a run of smaller single-feature commits (adf13e8 offline outbox + .dpwq handover, c9b080e questionnaire reuse, 4c0fed1 questionnaire reference + product→process cascade, 3bccb47 report units, e021263 QR join grants, 83608e2 declared text formats, f97726a sketch trace downloads/framing/sharpening/comparison, 102560b the 285th default question, efe2b1a masked Aadhaar). Roughly 45 distinct user-visible capabilities landed, on the web, on Android, or on both. The critical fact for the downstream question: docs/WALKTHROUGH.md has not been edited since a9adb47, so the two largest waves (7b78836 and 3def0f6) are entirely absent from it; frontend/components/guide/steps.ts was last edited in 7b78836 and only for the designer-profile required-fields bullet, so 3def0f6 is entirely absent from the in-app guide; and frontend/app/page.tsx has not changed since the initial commit, with frontend/components/hero/ last touched in 7b78836 for three small copy edits. One shipped doc is now factually wrong: docs/SKETCHES-PROTOTYPES-PARITY.md:110 still records web PDF/EPS/DXF export as an Android-only gap, while frontend/components/sketches/upload/traceExport.ts:141 now offers all five formats.

### Gaps — what the requirement asks for that is not there

- **docs/WALKTHROUGH.md has not been edited since a9adb47 — three commits and two entire feature waves ago. Nothing from 7b78836 (inspector tier, paginated A4 preview, photo gate and 25-photo galleries, media picker, grid measurement on record forms, designer team, two survey charts, native Android tracer) or 3def0f6 (workshop-scoped records, the smart default, dictation everywhere, the language dropdown, the shared default questionnaire, offline questionnaire create, Measure-from-photograph on the web, the Sketches Upload/Review tabs on Android, unfiled records, Scan a code) appears in it.**
  - evidence: git log --oneline -- docs/WALKTHROUGH.md returns a9adb47, b7e3424, b967922 — a9adb47 is the newest. Word sweep over the file: "dictat" 0 hits, "Inspector" 0, "inspect" 0, "Scan a code" 0, "Measure a dimension" 0, "unfiled" 0.
  - change here: docs/WALKTHROUGH.md — the ten record-form steps (§1-§10, lines 90-344) need the workshop filing box and the microphone; §7 needs the Language dropdown and the shared/default form; §8 needs the media caption dictation; the design-workshop section (line 359 onward) needs new subsections for the inspector's read-only view, the photo gate, and the paginated preview. Its own "How this document is kept true" rule at line 620 is the thing that was not followed.

### Traps and risks

- THE NINETEEN-STEP COUNT IS ASSERTED IN THREE PLACES AND WILL BREAK IF ONLY ONE IS EDITED. frontend/app/(protected)/guide/page.tsx:42 and :47 render the count from GUIDE_STEPS.length (safe), but docs/WALKTHROUGH.md:16-24 hard-lists all nineteen ids by name and claims the two renderings are 'back in step as of 2026-08-26', and docs/tools/check-docs.mjs sweeps these files. Adding a guide step without adding the matching WALKTHROUGH section re-opens a debt the file explicitly records as paid.
- docs/REPO_FACTS.md IS GENERATED FROM DISK AND MUST BE REGENERATED AFTER THE COMMIT, NOT BEFORE. Six of the last twenty commits exist only to do this. It holds two column pairs — tracked files/lines via git, and tree files/lines off disk — so regenerating on a dirty tree passes locally and fails on a clean CI checkout where both pairs agree (this is exactly what 6c52af8 had to fix). Run node docs/tools/check-docs.mjs --write on a clean tree, after committing.
- docs/tools/check-docs.mjs ENFORCES A 'How this document is kept true' SECTION and a 'current provider is stated once' sweep across docs/. README.md:610, docs/WALKTHROUGH.md:620 and docs/KUBERNETES.md all carry one; a new doc without it, or a doc naming a database provider a second time, fails the docs check on the pull request. The four android/core-* modules are exempted by path prefix because they are vendored verbatim and hashed in android/UPSTREAM-MANIFEST-KOTLIN.txt — never edit a line in them to satisfy a docs rule.
- THE TWO CLIENTS' DASHBOARD GRIDS ARE HELD TO EACH OTHER BY TEST. frontend/e2e/dashboard-tile-parity-unit.spec.ts and android/.../DashboardTileParityTest.kt mean a tile cannot be added on the web without being added on the handset in the same change. Labels are also compared verbatim ('Scan a code', 'Design review', 'Sketches & prototypes'), so any copy edit to a tile label must land on both clients.
- THE SKETCHES PARITY MATRIX IS A MAINTAINED ARTEFACT, NOT PROSE. docs/SKETCHES-PROTOTYPES-PARITY.md rows carry per-row 'Verified <date>; re-check with <grep>' instructions and a four-value verdict vocabulary (BOTH / WEB ONLY (deliberate) / WEB ONLY (gap) / ANDROID ONLY (…)). Row 110 is already stale. Editing it means re-running the row's own grep and re-stamping the date, not just flipping the word.
- DICTATION IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT FROM THREE BOX KINDS ON BOTH CLIENTS — a name, a code and a number — because a recogniser returns the nearest dictionary word and the respondent's name is the string sittings are searched by. Any landing-page or walkthrough copy claiming 'a microphone on every box' would be false; the shipped claim in the commit is 'a microphone on every record page' and the guide's existing wording is 'A microphone on every narrative box' (frontend/components/guide/steps.ts:597). Reuse that wording.
- THE INSPECTOR TIER IS NOT 'INSPECTOR AND ABOVE'. frontend/lib/permissions.ts:842 defines INSPECTION_ROLES as exactly frozenset({'INSPECTOR'}), and permissions.ts:822-842 warns that rank 37 sitting between DESIGNER and PROFESSOR misleads every threshold instinct. Copy describing the inspector as a senior designer, or as something a professor also gets, would be wrong. permissions.ts:220 also records that twenty-two hand-kept copies of the ladder had to be corrected when INSPECTOR landed — the landing page's AccessLadder derives its count from TIERS_LOW_TO_HIGH.length precisely so it cannot drift again.
- MCP NOTE: the codegraph MCP server failed to connect this session and the codegraph CLI was not on PATH, so every claim above was verified by reading files and git directly rather than through the index.

## 6. Existing feedback system (web /feedback, backend /feedback routes + Prisma Feedback model, Android FeedbackScreen + MasterFeedbackCard) — reconnaissance for Requirements 21, 24, 26 (research/patent-grade user feedback collection)

**Summary.** A complete, three-surface feedback feature already exists and is wired end to end: a web page at frontend/app/(protected)/feedback/page.tsx, an Android screen at MainActivity.kt:10824, and three FastAPI routes in backend/app/api/routes/feedback.py backed by a single Prisma `Feedback` table (schema.prisma:349-373). It is an APP-SATISFACTION SURVEY, not a research instrument: seven 1-5 star ratings (one overall plus six fixed aspects — ease of use, reliability, performance, design, features, recommend), five free-text prompts (likeMost, improve, bugs, featureRequests, comment) and one free-text self-described `role`. Every field is optional; the only validation is "at least one answer" enforced client-side on both platforms (page.tsx:226-235, MainActivity.kt:10929-10936), plus Pydantic bounds ge=1/le=5 and max_length=5000/200 (schemas/feedback.py:12-25). Storage is ONE ROW PER USER, upserted on `userId` (@unique at schema.prisma:351; upsert at feedback.py:98-104) — resubmitting OVERWRITES the previous answers, so there is no submission history at all. Only MASTER_ADMIN can read others' feedback (`require_master_admin`, feedback.py:111; deps.py:698-704); the web renders the list inline on the same /feedback page (page.tsx:339-368) and Android as an admin-hub card (MainActivity.kt:10996). Android has full parity — its wording is the source the web copied verbatim (page.tsx:40, :51-55, :89). For a research/patent instrument the gaps are structural, not cosmetic: no task context, no target feature/screen, no session or record linkage, no per-response history, no captured account role, no platform/app version, no consent field, no validated scale, no export.

### What exists today

- Web feedback page exists as a client component at the protected route /feedback, default export FeedbackPage.

- The six aspect ratings are defined in RATING_FIELDS with exact labels: easeOfUse "Ease of use", reliability "Reliability / stability", performance "Speed / performance", design "Design / look & feel", features "Features / completeness", recommend "How likely you'd recommend it". A comment states these are in the Android screen's order and with its exact wording.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/feedback/page.tsx:40-48`
- A seventh, separate overall rating is labelled "Overall rating" and is rendered above the aspect rows; the word under it comes from OVERALL_WORDS = ["Tap a star to rate", "Poor", "Fair", "Good", "Very good", "Excellent"], indexed by the rating.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/feedback/page.tsx:90,277-281`
- All ratings are 1-5 star rows; clicking the current value clears it back to null ("I would rather not answer this one" stays reachable). The trailing text reads `${value}/5` or "Not rated".
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/feedback/page.tsx:118-131`
- The five free-text prompts and their exact labels/placeholders: likeMost "What do you like most?" / "The part of the app you would not want taken away."; improve "What should we improve?" / "The step that takes longest, or the screen you avoid."; bugs "Any bugs or issues you hit?" / "What you were doing, what you expected, and what happened instead."; featureRequests "Features you'd like to see" / "Something you currently do on paper, or in another app."; comment "Anything else (general comments)" / "Anything the questions above did not cover."
