- `F:/Portal_Development_Designer/backend/app/api/routes/artisans.py`
- `F:/Portal_Development_Designer/backend/prisma/migrations/20260816170000_artisan_dob_and_experience/migration.sql`

### Traps and risks

- STORAGE RECOMMENDATION — SPLIT BY MODEL, because the two fields are not the same shape. (A) DesignerProfile: you MUST add `experienceMonths Int?`. There is no date on that model to derive from (verified column-by-column at backend/prisma/schema.prisma:3946-4010), and you cannot recover months from an integer count of years. (B) Artisan: add `experienceMonths Int?` as well, but treat it exactly as `experienceYears` is treated today — the SECOND answer, behind `craftStartDate`. Where a row has a `craftStartDate`, derive BOTH dropdowns from it (a date already contains the month, so `derive_experience_months` is pure arithmetic on the same date, band 0..11, and it adds no new stored fact); where it does not, the two dropdowns write the stated pair. This preserves the existing precedence intact rather than competing with it.
- WHY NOT 'ONE NUMERIC COLUMN, DERIVE Y/M' — it works for Artisan-with-a-date and fails everywhere else. `Artisan.experienceYears Int?` is a whole-year count with no sub-year resolution, and it is the ONLY answer for every row written before 2026-08-23 (backend/prisma/migrations/20260823093000_artisan_craft_start_date/migration.sql:36-70 refuses to backfill a date precisely so those rows keep printing what they printed yesterday). Deriving months from it would always yield 0 months, which is a fabricated precision. For DesignerProfile it fails outright: no date exists to derive from.
- MIGRATION CONSEQUENCES — follow the 20260823093000 precedent exactly: one nullable additive column per table, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` (that file explains at :101-103 that several agents apply migrations against one local Postgres, so a re-run is realistic), and NO BACKFILL. A backfill of `experienceMonths = 0` would be a claim nobody made, on rows already inside filed ministry documents. Note the file's own warning: THERE IS NO DOWN-MIGRATION IN THIS PROJECT, and pushing main deploys with no test gate — so rollback is a manual `ALTER TABLE … DROP COLUMN`.
- DO NOT DISABLE OR DELETE THE EXISTING NUMBER BOX when a date is present. frontend/components/forms/ArtisanForm.tsx:1345-1354 documents a live trap: a disabled input is omitted from FormData, `submit` reads the absence as an explicit null, and `_CLEARABLE_COLUMNS` (backend/app/api/routes/artisans.py:167-168) makes the API clear the column — so clearing the joining date a week later would leave the artisan with no experience at all. android/…/MainActivity.kt:7973-7978 repeats the same warning. A disabled `<select>` behaves identically.
- BAND ENFORCEMENT IS MIRRORED IN 3-4 PLACES PER FIELD AND IS LOAD-BEARING. Artisan 0..90: backend/app/services/stage_definitions.py:806, backend/app/schemas/records.py:156 and :220, frontend/components/forms/ArtisanForm.tsx:1361-1363, android/…/MainActivity.kt:7971 (`.take(2)`), and the derivation's own band at backend/app/services/records.py:213. Designer 0..70: backend/app/services/stage_definitions.py:364, backend/app/schemas/designers.py:138, frontend/components/designers/DesignerProfileForm.tsx:464-465, android/…/DesignerProfileScreen.kt:680. A months dropdown must be 0..11 in every one of the corresponding places or a hydrated value gets refused on a box nobody typed in.
- `validate_entry` RE-COERCES EVERY FIELD ON EVERY SAVE, not just the ones a designer touched (documented at backend/app/services/records.py:182-189). An out-of-band hydrated months value would become a refused answer counted in `refusedAnswers` on a control the designer never opened. This is exactly why `derive_experience_years` returns None rather than an out-of-range number, and a months derivation must do the same.
- ZERO IS A REAL VALUE — use `is not None` / `_first_answer`, never `or`. This rule is stated at backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:1545-1547, backend/app/services/record_fields.py:452-454 and backend/app/services/records.py:190-194. `0 months` is the common case (anyone on an exact anniversary), so an `or` here would be wrong far more often than the years bug it was written about.
- HYDRATION FREEZES AT SAVE TIME AND THE REPORT NEVER RE-RESOLVES (backend/app/services/stage_definitions.py:836-847, and the migration's own correction of an earlier false claim at 20260823093000/migration.sql:20-33). A months value copied into `participant` is frozen on the day the artisan was picked, exactly as the years value is. Do not write help text promising it stays current — that mistake was already made once and had to be reverted.
- GUARD TESTS WILL FAIL THE BUILD ON A SLOPPY REGISTRY EDIT: `test_no_new_table_column_was_added_to_a_table_whose_widths_are_already_full` (backend/tests/test_reference_carry.py:4312) sums ALL TABLE_COLUMN widths on the entity, and `test_every_prefilled_profile_column_has_a_receiving_field` (backend/tests/test_designer_prefill_contract.py:95) resolves every PREFILL_MAP target against STAGES so a misspelt key cannot ship. Adding a designer months column means adding BOTH halves — the profile column and a receiving registry field.
- THE TWO WIRE ENCODERS ARE HAND-WRITTEN, NOT REFLECTIVE, so a new key that is not added to both will be silently dropped with no error: `fullDesignerProfileBody` in frontend/lib/designers.ts:438-455 (its docstring calls it "the full twenty-one-key wire body") and the Kotlin `put("experienceYears", …)` at android/…/ApiModels.kt:3204. The same applies to the two client-side reference-carry maps, frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts:1683-1684 and its server source backend/app/services/stage_schema.py:1024-1025.
- THE REPORT PRINTS `unit` APPENDED TO THE VALUE (backend/app/services/report_builder.py:374 and :412), so `unit="years"` currently yields "32 years". A Years+Months pair rendered as two separate registry INT fields would print "32 years" and "6 months" as two separate entries. If the intent is one printed phrase, that needs a deliberate decision at backend/app/services/stage_definitions.py:805 — and note the fourth carry invariant stated at :836-839: a unit-less source column must not land in a box that declares a unit, and vice versa.
- LABEL CHANGES RIPPLE INTO EXPORT HEADERS. `_f("Experience (years)", …)` at backend/app/services/record_fields.py:444 and `_f("Practising since", …)` at :442 are the literal column headers in the /export CSVs, the /data/report workbook, the dataset-zip details.txt and the data browser's info card. Renaming to "Experience" plus a months column changes files that go to a ministry.
- EDIT-HISTORY / COLLAB PANELS ALREADY RENDER THESE COLUMNS BY NAME and deliberately do NOT redact them (backend/app/api/routes/artisans.py:100-108, mirrored at android/…/RecordEditHistory.kt:100-108). A new months column inherits that visibility by default; the backend comment is explicit that widening the redaction set is an owner's call and must be made in `access.REVISION_REDACTED_FIELDS` first, with the clients following — never on the handset alone.
- EXISTING TESTS PIN THE CURRENT SHAPE and will need updating: frontend/e2e/reference-hydration-unit.spec.ts (10 hits, including `field("experienceYears", "INT")` at :65 and `expect(typeof patch.experienceYears).toBe("number")` at :141), frontend/e2e/record-derivations-unit.spec.ts (5 hits, a golden-case table keyed `"age" | "experience"` at :43), frontend/e2e/reference-repoint-clearing.spec.ts (5 hits), backend/tests/test_reference_carry.py:1464, and android/app/src/test/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/RecordDerivationTest.kt:107 (`experience is refused above ninety and accepted at ninety`).

---

## 11. Existing analytics / telemetry / behaviour recording (reqs 22-25)

**Summary.** The thing called "analytics" in this repo is NOT usage analytics. `GET /api/analytics/design-workshops` and `/admin/analytics` are a cross-workshop CONTENT comparison — design adoption rates, 3/6/12-month follow-up, cost-to-price ratios, recurring design opportunities — computed from `DwStageEntry` rows in three flat queries. They record nothing and observe no user. What the backend does record is a write-audit: `RecordRevision` (one row per edit, `{field: {old, new}}`) written by exactly one function, and `ReviewLog` (one row per review decision or reviewer edit). Neither covers the 22-stage design workshop, which is audited only by a sparse `DwStageEntry.fieldProvenance` blob naming who last set each field with no prior value. There is ZERO page-view, screen-view, session, or navigation tracking anywhere — no table, no endpoint, no client code, on web or Android. There is no `lastSeenAt` on `User`; the only sign-in signal is a write-once `firstSeenAt` on two roster tables, and `deps.py:778` incorrectly claims it can tell you who has STOPPED using the app. The `/activity` page is not an event feed — it is a live re-query of six list endpoints filtered by `createdBy`, capped at 100 rows per type.

### What exists today

- The analytics route is a cross-workshop CONTENT comparison, not usage telemetry. One endpoint, read-only, admin-gated: `@router.get("/design-workshops")` / `async def design_workshop_analytics(_: Any = Depends(require_admin))`. Its module docstring opens "The cross-workshop comparison, read-only and admin-only." It writes nothing.
  - `backend/app/api/routes/analytics.py:169-180`
- It reads exactly three queries, never one per workshop: `db.designworkshop.count(where={"deletedAt": None})` and `db.dwstageentry.find_many(where={"deletedAt": None, "entityKey": {"in": [...]}}, take=ROW_CAP + 1, order={"id": "asc"})` gathered together, then `db.designworkshop.find_many(where={"id": {"in": workshop_ids}, "deletedAt": None})`.
  - `backend/app/api/routes/analytics.py:99-127`
- It reads only three entity keys — `followUp`, `costSheet`, `designOpportunity` — with `ROW_CAP = 50_000` and `HEADER_CAP = 20_000`, and a `truncated` flag rides to the payload so a partial read is never presented as a total.
  - `backend/app/api/routes/analytics.py:46-63`
- SERVER-SIDE AUDIT OF WRITES, MECHANISM 1 — `RecordRevision`. Table fields: `id`, `recordType String`, `recordId String`, `editedById String?`, `changes Json`, `createdAt DateTime @default(now())`; indexed `@@index([recordType, recordId])` and `@@index([editedById])`. No index on `createdAt`.
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:1820-1832`
- `RecordRevision` has exactly ONE writer: `access.record_revision`, which diffs the cleaned payload against the row, skips `REVISION_SKIP_FIELDS`, redacts `REVISION_REDACTED_FIELDS` to direction-only, returns early when nothing changed, and calls `db.recordrevision.create(data={"recordType": record_type.lower(), "recordId": ..., "editedById": ..., "changes": Json(changes)})`.
  - `backend/app/services/access.py:404-435`
  - also: REVISION_SKIP_FIELDS at access.py:131; REVISION_REDACTED_FIELDS at access.py:313
- `guard_record_edit` ends in `await record_revision(record, user, data, record_type)`, so every authorised field edit on the six record types is audited. Call sites: artisans.py:485, products.py:370, tools.py:389, crafts.py:176, processes.py:489, questionnaire.py:1145, workshops.py:752; plus direct `record_revision` calls at workshops.py:750, processes.py:511, review.py:511.
  - `backend/app/services/access.py:438-467`
- The write-audit is READ BACK by one endpoint only: `GET /api/data/revisions?recordType=&recordId=`, gated to the record's owner or an admin, ordered `createdAt asc`. It is per-record — there is no query by user, by time window, or across records.
  - `backend/app/api/routes/data_access.py:403-428`
- SERVER-SIDE AUDIT OF WRITES, MECHANISM 2 — `ReviewLog`: `id`, `recordType ReviewRecordType`, `recordId`, `status RecordStatus`, `notes String?`, `reviewerId`, `createdAt`; indexed on `[recordType, recordId]`, `[reviewerId]` and `[createdAt]`.
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:1516-1531`
- `ReviewLog` has two writers: `set_review_status` (approve/reject/revise) and the reviewer-edit path, whose note is prefixed `EDITED` because "ReviewLog has NO action column: its only verb is `status`".
  - `backend/app/api/routes/review.py:309-318`
  - also: second writer at review.py:521-531; EDIT_LOG_PREFIX at review.py:49-55
- THE 22-STAGE DESIGN WORKSHOP IS NOT COVERED BY EITHER. `DwStageEntry` carries `fieldProvenance Json?` — sparse `{fieldKey: {by, byName, at, source, refModel, refId, refKey}}` naming who LAST set each field. It is a current-author pointer, not a history: no old value, no per-edit row. `createdById` "stops being the truth the moment a second designer touches it".
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:2839-2864`
- No `record_revision` / `guard_record_edit` call exists anywhere in `backend/app/services/design_workshops.py` or `backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py` — verified by grep across all of backend/app. The stage-save path is unaudited by the revision ledger.
  - `backend/app/services/entry_provenance.py:23-38`
- GENUINE PER-USER USAGE METERS EXIST, but only for AI spend. `DwDictationDailyUsage` is `@@id([userId, istDay])` with a `count Int` of uploads that reached a transcription provider — described in its own comment as "a meter reading".
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:3580-3606`
- `DwAiVerbDailyUsage` is `@@id([userId, istDay, verb])` with a `count` per verb (PROOFREAD / EXPAND / TRANSLATE / CAPTION / SUBTITLES). Its comment states it "is also the only measurement of what these verbs cost that this system will ever have".
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:3631-3670`
- `DwReportExport` is the one true per-user FEATURE-USE event table: `format`, `templateId`, `fileName`, `fileSizeBytes`, `pageCount`, `generatedOnDevice Boolean`, `schemaVersion`, `generatedAt DateTime @default(now())`, `generatedById`; indexed `[designWorkshopId, generatedAt]` and `[generatedById]`.
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:3080-3106`
- Three further append-only decision ledgers exist, each scoped to one act rather than to behaviour: `DwAiLayerDecision` (layerId, decision, note, actorId, createdAt), `DwWorkshopConsentDecision`, and `RecordAccessTokenRedemption` (outcome, reason, scannedAtClient, monotonic client clocks). `DwWorkshopConsentDecision`'s comment says "Nothing reads this table back over HTTP except the response to the consent call itself."
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:3468-3496`
  - also: consent log at schema.prisma:3502-3512; redemption at schema.prisma:2677-2700
- SIGN-IN SIGNALS. `AccessRoster` carries `joinedAt`, `requestedAt`, `attemptCount Int @default(0)`, `lastAttemptAt` (REFUSED attempts only — "one person hammering the form is one row with a rising count"), `decidedAt`, and `firstSeenAt` = "First SUCCESSFUL sign-in after admission."
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:4043-4110`
- `firstSeenAt` is WRITE-ONCE and can never say who is still active: `mark_roster_seen` runs `db.designerroster.update_many(where={"email": address, "firstSeenAt": None}, data={"firstSeenAt": ...})` and its docstring states it "records the FIRST sign-in rather than the most recent one".
  - `backend/app/services/designers.py:97-116`
- The `User` model has NO `lastLoginAt`, `lastSeenAt`, `loginCount`, or session relation — only `createdAt DateTime @default(now())` and `updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt` alongside role/permission flags and ~60 authorship relations.
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:171-296`
- THE FASTAPI MIDDLEWARE STACK IS FOUR ENTRIES AND NONE INSTRUMENTS. In add-order (Starlette runs the last-added outermost): `app.add_middleware(UnhandledErrorMiddleware)`, `install_rate_limit(app)`, `CORSMiddleware`, `SecurityHeadersMiddleware`, `app.add_middleware(SelectiveGZipMiddleware)`, then `app.include_router(api_router)`.
  - `backend/app/main.py:560-682`
  - also: classes at main.py:247, 353, 474
- The rate limiter is the only middleware that already derives a per-request identity (`_identity(scope, ignore_bearer=credential)`) — but it is OFF by default: `scale_rate_limit_enabled: bool = Field(default=False, alias="SCALE_RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED")`.
  - `backend/app/core/config.py:385`
  - also: identity derivation at backend/app/scale/rate_limit.py:239; install at rate_limit.py:326-351
- THE ADMIN PAGE fires exactly one request on mount and computes nothing: `loadWorkshopAnalytics().then(...)`, where that helper is `return apiFetch<WorkshopAnalytics>("/analytics/design-workshops")`. The page's own rule 1 is "**Nothing here computes.** Every figure and every sentence comes off the wire."
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/admin/analytics/page.tsx:236-250`
  - also: helper at frontend/lib/workshopAnalytics.ts:162-164; rules at page.tsx:16-27
- Its rendered metrics, in order: cautions banner "Read these before the numbers" (:325); four coverage tiles "Workshops in the archive" / "With any follow-up" / "With cost sheets" / "Naming design opportunities" (:341-356); "What was set aside, and why" (:368); Adoption (:384) with tables "By craft" (:410), "By cluster" (:418), "By state" (:426); "At 3, 6 and 12 months" (:436); cost-to-price per cluster (:489); recurring design opportunities (:563); "What came back" — Revenue recorded / Units sold / Orders received (:643-657); "What this cannot tell you" (:667).
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/admin/analytics/page.tsx:325-667`
- Every figure and message string is computed server-side by `analyse_archive` and serialised by `archive_findings_payload`. Withholding floors are constants there: `MIN_WORKSHOPS_FOR_RATE = 5`, `MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_RATE = 8`, `MIN_WORKSHOPS_FOR_THEME = 2`.
  - `backend/app/services/workshop_analytics.py:109-129`
  - also: analyse_archive at :901; archive_findings_payload at :1080
- `rate: null` is a REFUSAL, never a zero, and is rendered as a bare em dash: `return group.rate === null ? "—" : `${Math.round(group.rate * 100)}%``.
  - `frontend/lib/workshopAnalytics.ts:150-152`
- The gate is mirrored in three places, all pointing at one server rule: `Depends(require_admin)` on the route, `ROUTE_GUARDS` entry for `/admin/analytics` with `can: isAdmin, gate: "require_admin"`, and `const permitted = isAdmin(user)` in the component. The nav entry is `{ href: "/admin/analytics", label: "Cross-workshop analytics", icon: ChartNoAxesCombined, group: "Admin", can: isAdmin, gate: "require_admin", adminSurface: true }`.
  - `frontend/lib/permissions.ts:352-373`
  - also: nav entry at frontend/components/DynamicIslandNav.tsx:419-424; component check at page.tsx:227-228
- THE ACTIVITY PAGE IS NOT AN EVENT FEED. It re-queries six list endpoints plus media and the user directory on every person-switch, filtered server-side by `const mine = { pageSize, createdBy: activeUserId }` with `pageSize = 100`. Nothing reads any event or audit table.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/activity/page.tsx:102-118`
- It only ever shows CREATED records — artisans, products, tools, processes, workshops, crafts, questionnaire interviews, media — each row reduced to `{ id, name, createdAt }`. No edits, no reads, no navigation, no sessions.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/activity/page.tsx:26-27`
  - also: groups built at page.tsx:168-222
- The only "does the tool help them" data that exists is SELF-REPORT: `Feedback`, one upserted row per user (`userId String @unique`), with `rating`/`easeOfUse`/`reliability`/`performance`/`design`/`features`/`recommend` each 1-5, plus free text `comment`/`likeMost`/`improve`/`bugs`/`featureRequests`/`role`.
  - `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:349-374`
  - also: routes at backend/app/api/routes/feedback.py:84, :91, :108
- uvicorn runs with DEFAULT access logging (no `--no-access-log`, no `--log-level` override) on both deployment targets, so an unstructured per-request line does reach journald / pod stdout: `ExecStart=... -m uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000 --workers 1`.
  - `infra/terraform/user_data.sh:78`
  - also: k8s args at infra/k8s/base/deployment-api.yaml:102-118

### Gaps — what the requirement asks for that is not there

- **NO client-side navigation or interaction tracking exists on the WEB client. Definitive no.**
  - evidence: frontend/package.json:14-36 lists no analytics SDK (no posthog, mixpanel, segment, amplitude, sentry, GA/gtag). A repo-wide grep of frontend for `gtag|dataLayer|posthog|mixpanel|amplitude|segment\.|sentry|analytics\.track|trackEvent|logEvent|pageview|page_view|navigator\.sendBeacon` returned only false positives on locals named `segment`/`amplitude`. frontend/app/layout.tsx has no analytics <Script>. `usePathname()` appears only for nav highlighting (frontend/components/AppShell.tsx:25, frontend/components/DynamicIslandNav.tsx:485) and route-guard matching (frontend/lib/permissions.ts:651-670) — it feeds no request. frontend/lib/api.ts contains no `performance.*`, `reportWebVitals`, or instrumentation hook.
  - change here: A new `frontend/lib/telemetry.ts` plus a client component mounted in frontend/app/(protected)/layout.tsx that watches `usePathname()`. Note AppShell.tsx:25 already holds the pathname.
- **NO client-side navigation or interaction tracking exists on ANDROID. Definitive no, and the app has essentially no logging at all.**
  - evidence: A grep of all android *.kt/*.kts/*.gradle/*.xml/*.toml for `firebase|Analytics|analytics|Telemetry|telemetry|crashlytics|logEvent|trackScreen|Timber` returned ONE hit, and it is a comment recording a deliberate decision: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/ApiKeysScreen.kt:102 — "crash report or an analytics event — there is not one logging call in this file." Across the whole main source tree there are 2 `Log.*` calls total (DwAsrSpeechModel.kt) and zero `addOnDestinationChangedListener` / `currentBackStackEntry` observers.
  - change here: Greenfield. Would need a new module plus a NavController destination listener in the app's navigation host.
  - android twin: NONE — there is no analytics, telemetry, or logging module on Android to extend.
- **NO page-view / screen-view / navigation record is stored server-side either. No table, no endpoint, no column.**
  - evidence: All 59 Prisma models enumerated by `grep -n '^model ' backend/prisma/schema.prisma` — there is no Audit*, Event*, PageView*, Session*, Visit*, Telemetry*, or Track* model. The only names matching audit/usage semantics are `ReviewLog` (schema.prisma:1516), `RecordRevision` (:1820), `DwDictationDailyUsage` (:3580) and `DwAiVerbDailyUsage` (:3631). The router mounts 38 route modules and none is named events/telemetry/usage/tracking (backend/app/api/router.py:3-38).
  - change here: A new Prisma model + migration, a new `backend/app/api/routes/` module registered in backend/app/api/router.py, and an ingest path.
- **"How often does anyone use this" is unanswerable — there is no last-seen anywhere. `firstSeenAt` is write-once on both roster tables and stamps only the FIRST sign-in.**
  - evidence: backend/app/services/designers.py:113-116 uses `where={"email": address, "firstSeenAt": None}`, and its docstring (designers.py:101-104) says it "records the FIRST sign-in rather than the most recent one". backend/app/services/access_roster.py:426-430 does the same with an explicit `if row is None or getattr(row, "firstSeenAt", None) is not None: return`. backend/prisma/schema.prisma:171-296 shows `User` has no lastSeenAt/lastLoginAt.
  - change here: backend/prisma/schema.prisma User model (new column + migration); the sign-in path in backend/app/api/routes/auth.py that already calls `mark_roster_seen`.
- **A LIVE DOCUMENTATION DEFECT: a dependency docstring claims `firstSeenAt` reveals who has stopped using the app. It cannot.**
  - evidence: backend/app/core/deps.py:778 reads "A designer able to GET it would be reading their colleagues' standing — and, from ``firstSeenAt``, which of them has stopped using the app." That is contradicted by backend/app/services/designers.py:101-104 and by the write-once WHERE clause at designers.py:113-116. `firstSeenAt` can only distinguish 'never arrived' from 'arrived at least once'.
  - change here: backend/app/core/deps.py:772-785 (`require_designer_roster_manager` docstring) — correct the claim, or add the lastSeenAt column that would make it true.
  - android twin: NONE
- **WRITE-AUDIT COVERAGE IS INCOMPLETE on the product's flagship surface. `RecordRevision` covers artisan/product/tool/craft/process/questionnaire/workshop, but NOT design-workshop stage saves.**
  - evidence: guard_record_edit call sites are exhaustively: artisans.py:485, products.py:370, tools.py:389, crafts.py:176, processes.py:489, questionnaire.py:1145, workshops.py:752; direct record_revision at workshops.py:750, processes.py:511, review.py:511. None is in design_workshops. backend/prisma/schema.prisma:2856-2860 confirms the substitute is a sparse `fieldProvenance` blob and that backfilling it "would manufacture an audit trail on a document submitted to a ministry".
  - change here: backend/app/services/design_workshops.py `save_stage_data` / `save_stage`; backend/app/services/entry_provenance.py.
- **NO READ is recorded anywhere. Every audit mechanism is write-only, so "who looked at what" — the core of navigation mapping — has no data at all.**
  - evidence: backend/app/services/access.py:404-435 diffs a write payload; backend/app/api/routes/review.py:309 and :521 log decisions and edits. `list_revisions` (backend/app/api/routes/data_access.py:403-428) reads without logging. The analytics route itself declares "it issues three SELECTs and writes nothing" (backend/app/api/routes/analytics.py:176-178).
  - change here: A new ASGI middleware between backend/app/main.py:584 and :585, or a shared dependency layered onto backend/app/core/deps.py:624.
- **NO per-request instrumentation point is occupied. The exact slot is identified and empty.**
  - evidence: backend/app/main.py:560-620 registers four middleware and not one records anything: UnhandledErrorMiddleware only logs unhandled exceptions (main.py:290), SecurityHeadersMiddleware stamps headers, SelectiveGZipMiddleware compresses. The rate limiter that would sit at the same position is disabled by default (backend/app/core/config.py:385).
  - change here: Insert a pure-ASGI class registered between `install_rate_limit(app)` (main.py:584) and `app.add_middleware(CORSMiddleware, ...)` (main.py:585) — inside CORS, outside the router. For per-USER attribution the DI point is `get_current_user` (backend/app/core/deps.py:624-628), which every `require_*` funnels through (deps.py:692, 698, 707, 713, 722, 745, 750, 759, 772, 788, 803, 906).
- **NO client-platform marker (web vs Android) is stored on any behaviour row, so "how they navigate" cannot be split by device.**
  - evidence: The only two platform-aware columns in the whole schema are `DwReportExport.generatedOnDevice Boolean @default(false)` (backend/prisma/schema.prisma:3089-3091) and `RecordAccessTokenRedemption.scannedAtClient`/monotonic clocks (schema.prisma:2686-2696). Neither is attached to a request or a screen.
  - change here: Any new event model; plus a client header set in frontend/lib/api.ts and the Android network layer.
- **NO time-on-task or dwell measurement. `DwStageEntry` gives first and last write per row and nothing between.**
  - evidence: backend/prisma/schema.prisma:2893-2896 — `createdAt DateTime @default(now())`, `updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt`, `createdById String?`, `deletedAt DateTime?`. `fieldProvenance` carries a per-field `at` (schema.prisma:2839-2842) but that is a last-write stamp, not an interval.
  - change here: A new event model would be required; the stage-save path in backend/app/services/design_workshops.py is where a start/end pair could be stamped.
- **The `/activity` page renders the "Processes" group TWICE — two identical objects are pushed into the same array.**
  - evidence: frontend/app/(protected)/activity/page.tsx:192-204 contains two consecutive object literals both with `title: "Processes"`, `href: "/processes"` and the same `by(raw.processes)` mapping (the duplicates begin at page.tsx:194 and page.tsx:200). Both survive the trailing `.filter((group) => group.rows.length > 0)`, so any user with at least one process sees the section rendered twice.
  - change here: frontend/app/(protected)/activity/page.tsx:198-204 — delete the second literal.
  - android twin: Android's My Activity screen (referenced as the IA owner at frontend/app/(protected)/activity/page.tsx:55-60) lists Processes once.
- **The name "analytics" is already taken by the content feature, at every layer, so a usage-analytics feature cannot reuse it without collision.**
  - evidence: Backend prefix `router = APIRouter(prefix="/analytics", tags=["analytics"])` (backend/app/api/routes/analytics.py:41); web route `/admin/analytics` (frontend/app/(protected)/admin/analytics/page.tsx); guard entry `path: "/admin/analytics"` (frontend/lib/permissions.ts:367); nav label "Cross-workshop analytics" (frontend/components/DynamicIslandNav.tsx:424); client helper `frontend/lib/workshopAnalytics.ts:162`.
  - change here: Pick a distinct prefix (e.g. /usage or /telemetry) in backend/app/api/router.py and a distinct route under frontend/app/(protected)/admin/.

### Traps and risks

- MIDDLEWARE POSITION IS LOAD-BEARING AND ARGUED IN COMMENTS. A new instrumentation middleware must be added between backend/app/main.py:584 and :585 — inside CORS, outside the router. main.py:562-583 records that a response produced OUTSIDE CORS reaches the browser with no `access-control-allow-origin`, so `fetch` rejects and the web app says "Failed to fetch" while Android sees the honest status. main.py:615-620 records that SelectiveGZipMiddleware must stay outermost because it is the last thing to touch `content-length`. Getting this wrong reproduces two documented production incidents.
- A DB WRITE PER REQUEST WOULD FIGHT AN EXISTING CONCURRENCY BUDGET. `gather_reads` is bounded by `pool_width()` = `DATABASE_CONNECTION_LIMIT` = 10, and backend/app/api/routes/dashboard.py:88-101 explicitly warns "do not raise DATABASE_CONNECTION_LIMIT to fit it, which is the mistake the 40 -> 10 cut recorded in core/config.py was reverting". An event row per request needs batching or a separate sink, not a synchronous insert.
- ASGI MIDDLEWARE CANNOT SEE THE USER AND THE DI POINT CANNOT SEE THE RESPONSE. Middleware runs outside the router and never decodes the JWT, so it yields path/method/status/duration with no identity. `get_current_user` (backend/app/core/deps.py:624-628) yields the identity but runs before the handler, so it knows neither the status code nor the duration. Any real instrumentation needs both halves stitched via `scope["state"]`.
- REDACTION DISCIPLINE MUST BE INHERITED. `access.REVISION_REDACTED_FIELDS` (backend/app/services/access.py:313) exists because "a retraction which copies the retracted value into an append-only table is not a retraction" (data_access.py:414-419). Any new append-only behaviour table that logs request paths would capture record ids and query strings and must apply the same rule. Note the API already sets `referrer-policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` for exactly that reason (backend/app/main.py:181-186).
- BEHAVIOUR RECORDING OF DESIGNERS IS A NEW PERSONAL-DATA CATEGORY WITH NO CONSENT PATH. This codebase already models consent explicitly and per-workshop for audio (`DwDictationConsent`, three states not a boolean, backend/prisma/schema.prisma:3530-3556) and keeps a permanent decision log (`DwWorkshopConsentDecision`). There is no equivalent for observing designers, and the roster docstring at backend/app/core/deps.py:772-779 treats "which of them has stopped using the app" as sensitive enough to gate the READ of the roster to admins.
- THE ANALYTICS `_load` READ IS TUNED AND MUST NOT BE EXTENDED. backend/app/api/routes/analytics.py:66-98 documents measured timings (25.7 ms end to end vs 242 ms for the per-workshop shape) and depends on `@@index([entityKey])` — "the one index on DwStageEntry that does NOT lead with designWorkshopId". Adding usage queries into this endpoint would re-argue a trade the schema already settled.
- THE PAGE MUST NOT COMPUTE. frontend/app/(protected)/admin/analytics/page.tsx:16-21 states rule 1: "Nothing here computes… `adoptionRateText` is the only renderer of a rate", and frontend/lib/workshopAnalytics.ts:15-21 warns that `null` coerces to 0 through arithmetic and through `??`. Any new metric added to this page must arrive pre-formatted from the server with its own withholding decision already made.
- THE GATE IS MIRRORED IN THREE PLACES AND THE REPO HAS SHIPPED THE UI-ONLY VERSION TWICE. backend/app/api/routes/analytics.py:14-18 records "This repository has twice shipped a UI guard over an open endpoint — the link disappeared and the URL stayed open". A usage-analytics page needs the server dependency FIRST, then frontend/lib/permissions.ts ROUTE_GUARDS, then the component check — in that order.
- ANDROID INSTRUMENTATION IS FULLY GREENFIELD AND ONE SCREEN HAS AN EXPLICIT NO-LOG DECISION. There is no Timber, no logger abstraction, and 2 `Log.*` calls in the entire main source tree. android/.../ui/ApiKeysScreen.kt:102 deliberately records that there is not one logging call in that file — any blanket screen-view instrumentation must respect that exemption.

---

## 12. Design prototype workshop editing (Requirement 27) — web record page, create flow, backend PATCH, Android twin, house edit-route convention

**Summary.** Requirement 27 is CONFIRMED for the web client. There is no `/design-workshops/[id]/edit` route and no edit affordance anywhere on the record page: the record page's header offers nine links (Cards & tags, Sketches & prototypes, Import photographs, AI layers, This workshop's own questions, Ready to submit?, Report history, Authorship & divergence, Report) and none of them edits the workshop entity. The only write to the DesignWorkshop row from that page is `patchDesignWorkshop(workshopId, { status })` inside SubmissionCard — status and nothing else. Everything else editable from the record page belongs to CHILDREN of the workshop (22 stages, photos, codes, custom sections, AI layers, dictation consent, report). The workshop's own cover attributes (title, craft, cluster, state, district, venue, dates, scheme, agency, sponsor, workshop code) are reachable today ONLY by opening stage 1 `WORKSHOP_SETUP` and saving it, because those are "promoted columns" written by the stage save. Three fields — `notes`, `workshopId` (the link to a Workshop record) and `templateId` — have no stage-1 twin at all and are therefore write-once at create time in the web UI. The backend already has a full `PATCH /design-workshops/{id}` accepting eleven fields, so this is a pure client gap. Android is worse: its Retrofit interface has no PATCH for design workshops at all, so a handset can neither edit the header nor set a status.

### What exists today

- There is NO edit route for design workshops. The complete subroute set under the record page is: ai-layers, codes, custom-sections, page.tsx, photos, provenance, readiness, report/history, report (+ reportTarget.ts), sketches-and-prototypes, stages/[stageKey]. `find frontend/app -type d -name edit` returns exactly three: artisans/[id]/edit, products/[id]/edit, tools/[id]/edit — design-workshops is not among them, and there is no `new` route for it either.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:1 (directory listing of frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/)`
- A repo-wide grep for any href matching `design-workshops/…edit` returns no matches — nothing anywhere in frontend/ links to a design-workshop edit screen.
  - `frontend/lib/workshopCodeLookup.ts:90`
- The record page's header actions are exactly nine links and none is "Edit": `Cards & tags` → /codes, `Sketches & prototypes`, `Import photographs` → /photos, `AI layers`, `This workshop's own questions` → /custom-sections, `Ready to submit?` → /readiness, `Report history`, `Authorship & divergence` → /provenance (admin only), `Report`.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:1122-1206`
- The record page's own title/description are READ-ONLY renderings of the fetched detail: `title={detail?.title ?? "Design workshop"}` and a description built from `[detail.craftName, detail.clusterName, detail.state].filter(Boolean).join(" · ")`, falling back to the literal "Craft, cluster and place are filled in from stage 1."
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:1113-1120`
- The record page's detail panel prints `workshopCode` and the date range as plain spans with no control, and the footnote states the rule out loud: "Craft, cluster, dates and the workshop code above are filled in from stage 1 and stay blank until it is saved."
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:1237-1285`
- The ONLY workshop-entity write on the record page is the status transition: `const updated = await patchDesignWorkshop(workshopId, { status: action.status });` inside `SubmissionCard.move()`. The card offers Mark complete / Submit / Reopen and is gated on `canRunDesignWorkshops(user)`.
  - `frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:571`
- `patchDraftHeader` is the only writer of the offline draft header and it has NO UI caller anywhere in the app. The store says so explicitly: "what kept that harmless was `headerDirtyAt: null` and the fact that nothing called {@link patchDraftHeader}. The first UI to call it on a workshop this browser had not read in full would have nulled the office's `notes` and overwritten its title with `""`, under a 200, with no screen saying anything." Grep confirms only doc-comment references plus the definition itself.

- The offline outbox already contains a working header-PATCH arm that sends only the keys recorded in `headerDirtyKeys`, from a fixed ten-key whole: title, templateId, craftName, clusterName, state, district, startDate, endDate, workshopId, notes. `status` is deliberately NOT among them.

- The record page states in a comment why status cannot go through the draft header: "the only writer of the draft header is `patchDraftHeader`, which stamps `headerDirtyAt` and arms the outbox's PATCH, and that PATCH sends a FIXED list of header fields with no `status` key in it… So this act is online-only by construction."

- The DPW CREATE flow lives inline on the LIST page, not on a `/new` route: an uncontrolled `<form ref={formRef} onSubmit={submit} onInput={markDirty}>` rendered when `allowCreate && formOpen`, headed "Start a design workshop" with the sub-line "Only the title is needed to begin. Everything else here is also asked in stage 1 and will be filled in from there — this is the shortcut for what is already known in the room." Toggled by a header button labelled "New design workshop"/"Close".

Buttons: "Create design workshop" / "Cancel".

Body keys: title, templateId, designerUserId, designerUserIds, craftName, clusterName, state, district, startDate, endDate, notes, workshopId.

- Backend PATCH EXISTS and is complete. `@router.patch("/{workshop_id}") async def update_design_workshop(workshop_id: str, payload: DesignWorkshopUpdate, current_user: Any = Depends(get_current_user)) -> dict[str, Any]` — guards `_require_designer(current_user)` then `load_workshop_or_404(workshop_id, current_user, for_edit=True)`; copies nine string/enum keys `("title", "templateId", "craftName", "clusterName", "state", "district", "notes", "workshopId", "status")` when not None (stripping strings), plus `startDate`/`endDate` through `_parse_date`; returns `workshop_summary(record)` unchanged when the body is empty.
  - `backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1648-1668`
- `DesignWorkshopUpdate` is "the all-optional mirror of DesignWorkshopCreate": title (min 1, max 220), templateId (max 48), craftName (160), clusterName (160), state (80), district (80), startDate, endDate, workshopId (64), notes (max MAX_NOTES_CHARS=20_000), status (24). A model validator rejects a status outside {DRAFT, IN_PROGRESS, COMPLETE, SUBMITTED, ARCHIVED} and a templateId outside the registered templates, each a 422 naming the allowed values. Its docstring: "the promoted columns (craft, cluster, dates …) are normally written by saving stage 1, not through this body… They are accepted here as well so an admin can correct a list entry without opening the stage."
  - `backend/app/schemas/design_workshops.py:217-259`
- Permission on PATCH: `_require_designer` = the SET {DESIGNER, ADMIN, MASTER_ADMIN} (`can_run_design_workshops`, a set and not a rank floor — a PROFESSOR is refused), and its docstring names PATCH explicitly as one of the eighteen routes it stands in front of. Row-level access is `load_workshop_or_404(..., for_edit=True)`: creator, admin, or holder of a `DesignWorkshopViewer` grant; anyone else gets 404 "Record not found"; a soft-deleted workshop gets 409 "This workshop is deleted. Restore it before editing."

- CREATE is gated far more narrowly than PATCH: `assert_can_create_design_workshops(current_user)` = `DESIGN_WORKSHOP_CREATOR_ROLES = frozenset({"ADMIN", "MASTER_ADMIN"})`. ### Gaps — what the requirement asks for that is not there

- **There is no `/design-workshops/[id]/edit` route and no edit control on the record page. A designer or admin cannot change the workshop's title, notes, report template, linked Workshop record, or its cover fields from the record page — the requirement as stated is TRUE.**
  - evidence: No `edit` directory under frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/; header actions carry nine links and none of them edits the entity (frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:1122-1206); the only entity write on the page is the status PATCH at frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:571
  - change here: New `frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/edit/page.tsx` following frontend/app/(protected)/tools/[id]/edit/page.tsx:1-41; a new `frontend/components/forms/DesignWorkshopForm.tsx` (or hoist the create form out of frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:903-1069 so create and edit share one component, matching ToolForm's `initial?:` convention); add an `Edit` / `Workshop details` link into the header `actions` at frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:1122-1206.
  - android twin: NONE — Android has no design-workshop edit screen and no PATCH route in android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepositoryApi.kt:1049-1058
- **Three fields are effectively WRITE-ONCE in the whole web product: `notes`, `workshopId` (the link to a Workshop record) and `templateId`. They are on the create form and accepted by PATCH, but they are NOT promoted columns, so stage 1 cannot reach them either. Once the create form is submitted, nothing in the UI can change them.**
  - evidence: `notes`/`workshopId` are explicitly excluded from stage-1 seeding — "Same for `notes` and `workshopId`, neither of which is a promoted column at all" (backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1584-1588); PROMOTED_COLUMNS carries neither (backend/app/services/stage_schema.py:883-897); the create form is the only surface that sets them (frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:589-604)
  - change here: The new edit form must include Notes (TextArea), Report template (Dropdown fed by `listReportTemplates`) and the "Start from a recorded workshop" link picker; body assembled exactly as frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:589-604 minus the two designer keys.
  - android twin: NONE — android/.../StageSchema.kt:2067-2190 has no `workshopId` member at all, and the create dialog sends only title/templateId/designers/craftName/clusterName (WorkshopListScreen.kt:1583-1604)
- **PATCH cannot reach six of the thirteen promoted columns: `venue`, `scheme`, `designerName`, `implementingAgency`, `sponsor`, `workshopCode`. They are returned by `workshop_summary` (so an edit form will be tempted to render them) but `DesignWorkshopUpdate` has no member for any of them and the PATCH loop does not name them.**
  - evidence: PATCH copies only title, templateId, craftName, clusterName, state, district, notes, workshopId, status, startDate, endDate (backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1656-1664); the schema declares no venue/scheme/designerName/implementingAgency/sponsor/workshopCode (backend/app/schemas/design_workshops.py:225-237); yet all six are serialised out (backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:239-244)
  - change here: Either render those six read-only with a link to `/design-workshops/{id}/stages/WORKSHOP_SETUP`, or extend `DesignWorkshopUpdate` (backend/app/schemas/design_workshops.py:217-237) and the key tuple at backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1656 — the latter creates a second writer for columns whose single-writer rule is stated at backend/app/services/stage_schema.py:872-882 and must be argued first.
  - android twin: NONE
- **`designerUserId` / `designerUserIds` cannot be changed after create, by design — but the product has no per-workshop surface for the team either. The viewers panel that does change it sits on the LIST page and is admin+adminMode only, so a designer opening a record page has no way to see or amend who is on the workshop.**
  - evidence: PATCH is explicitly closed to both keys — `DwUpdateBody = Omit<Partial<DwCreateBody>, "designerUserId" | "designerUserIds"> & { status?: string }` (frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts:746-748) and android/.../StageSchema.kt:2122-2124, 2179-2181; the only web editor is `<DesignWorkshopViewersPanel />` behind `isAdmin(user) && adminMode` on the list page (frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:1135-1173)
  - change here: If the edit page is to carry the team, mount `DesignWorkshopViewersPanel` (or a per-workshop variant of it) on the new edit route and drive it through `PUT /design-workshops/{id}/viewers` — never through PATCH, which will 422 with `extra_forbidden`.
  - android twin: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/WorkshopViewersScreen.kt — Android DOES have a per-workshop viewers screen; the web does not
- **There is no offline story for a header edit, even though the plumbing exists and has never been exercised. `patchDraftHeader` + the outbox's `needsHeader` arm are written, documented and dead — the store's own comment says the door "is not shut" and that the first UI caller would blank the office's title and notes on a workshop this browser had only opened rather than fully read.**
  - evidence: frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:466-483 ("nothing called patchDraftHeader"); the outbox arm at frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:4664-4708; grep for `patchDraftHeader` returns only doc references and the definition
  - change here: An edit form that wants parity with the rest of this screen must write through `patchDraftHeader(localId, patch)` (frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:1619-1630) rather than calling `patchDesignWorkshop` directly, and must be the first caller — so it has to guarantee the draft was hydrated by a full server read (`adoptServerDetail`) before it lets anyone type, or it will PATCH empty strings over server values.
  - android twin: NONE — no Android header-edit path exists to mirror
- **Android cannot edit a design workshop and cannot even set its status: no PATCH route is declared for `design-workshops/{id}`.**
  - evidence: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepositoryApi.kt:1049-1058 declares GET/POST/GET-one/DELETE only, while @PATCH exists for eleven other resources in the same file (lines 47, 173, 185, 201, 215, 264, 544, 756, 1754, 1807, 1990)
  - change here: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepositoryApi.kt (add `@PATCH("design-workshops/{id}")`), a `DesignWorkshopUpdateBody` beside `DesignWorkshopCreateBody` in data/StageSchema.kt:2067, a repository method in data/WorkshopRepository.kt, and an entry point on ui/designworkshop/StageIndexScreen.kt or WorkshopListScreen.kt.
  - android twin: android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepositoryApi.kt:1049-1058
- **The create form is the de-facto edit form template but it lives INLINE in the list page rather than in `components/forms/`, unlike every other record family. An implementer following house convention has nothing to import.**
  - evidence: The DPW create form is JSX inside `DesignWorkshopsPageBody` (frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:903-1070) with its own `submit`, `applySourceWorkshop`, `markDirty` and `guard` closures (lines 338-661); every other family has a standalone `components/forms/XForm.tsx` taking `initial?:` (frontend/components/forms/ToolForm.tsx:171-180)
  - change here: Extract frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:529-661 + 903-1070 into `frontend/components/forms/DesignWorkshopForm.tsx` with an `initial?: DwSummary` prop, mirroring ToolForm's create/edit switch at frontend/components/forms/ToolForm.tsx:681-685; the list page then renders `<DesignWorkshopForm />` and the new edit route `<DesignWorkshopForm initial={record} />`.

### Traps and risks

- THE PROMOTED-COLUMN TRAP, and it is the big one. Six of the eleven PATCH-able fields (craftName, clusterName, state, district, startDate, endDate) are ALSO promoted columns owned by stage 1. `_coerce_promoted` nulls every promoted column of a touched entity whose value is blank — so a designer who corrects the cluster on a new edit form and later opens stage 1 and saves with that box empty gets the correction silently NULLed under a 200 reading "Stage saved". This exact regression already shipped once and is written up at backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:3635-3643. `title` is the one exception, skipped because the column is NOT NULL (backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:4758-4776). An edit form MUST either warn about this or write stage 1's entry rather than the column.
- `title` is the reverse trap: the create route deliberately does NOT seed `workshopSetup.workshopTitle`, precisely so "a later PATCH of the workshop title could not reach it" be frozen — see backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1584-1588. So a title changed by PATCH stands until somebody types a different one into stage 1, at which point stage 1 wins. Two writers, no arbitration.
- PATCH is `extra="forbid"` (APIModel). Sending `designerUserId` or `designerUserIds` on the update body 422s the WHOLE request with `extra_forbidden` — frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts:726-748 says the `Omit` is documentation only and does not enforce it on the one call path that matters (`Object.fromEntries` widens to an index signature). Build the update body by naming keys, never by spreading a draft header.
- Do not route `status` through `patchDraftHeader`/the outbox. The outbox's `whole` has no `status` key, so the status would never be sent AND `headerDirtyAt` would stay set, which makes `adoptServerDetail` refuse to refresh the header from the server forever — freezing the status chip on this device's guess. Argued at frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:593-604 and 886-897.
- If the edit form writes through `patchDraftHeader`, it becomes the FIRST caller of a path documented as unsafe on a partially-read draft: `ensureDraft` seeds a header of empty strings and nulls for a workshop this browser has merely OPENED, and a PATCH built from that would blank the server's title and notes under a 200. `headerDirtyKeys` mitigates it but only for keys the user actually touched — frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:461-483.
- Permission asymmetry: CREATE is admin/master-admin only (`canCreateDesignWorkshops`), EDIT is the designer set (`canRunDesignWorkshops` = {DESIGNER, ADMIN, MASTER_ADMIN}) plus row access via creator/admin/viewer-grant. Gating the new edit route on `allowCreate` — the flag the list page already has in scope — would wrongly lock designers out of a route the server admits them to. Use `canRunDesignWorkshops` (frontend/lib/permissions.ts:770-772).
- A soft-deleted workshop answers 409 "This workshop is deleted. Restore it before editing." on any `for_edit=True` load, and the web already relies on 409 being rethrown so the workshop-level catch can print the right sentence (backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:179-205). An edit form that swallows 409 as a generic failure loses that.
- The record page is offline-first and reads the LOCAL IndexedDB draft before the server. An edit form dropped onto that route that fetches with a bare `apiFetch` (the artisans/tools convention) will show an empty form on a laptop with no signal, breaking the page's own promise printed in the amber banner at frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:1213-1226.
- Nothing in the repo tests a design-workshop edit surface, because none exists — `frontend/e2e/` has specs for the create race, the create gate and the draft store, but no edit spec. There is also no `?edit=` deep-link convention for this family (frontend/lib/workshopCodeLookup.ts:86-90 sends `designWorkshop` straight to `/design-workshops/{id}`), so adding a route means adding its `OPEN_HREF`/search/dashboard wiring too if it is meant to be discoverable from /search, /data and the dashboard the way the other three edit routes are.

---

## 13. Center of Excellence source — F:/cxa-portal (Requirement 17)

**Summary.** F:/cxa-portal IS the Centre of Excellence website, confirmed on five independent signals; the user's "CXA-CMS" guess was the Vercel deployment name (projectName "cxa-cms"), not the directory. It is a large, finished Next.js 15 / React 19 / Prisma / Postgres application that ships TWO surfaces from one deployment: a public institutional site under app/(site) (~34 public routes) and a full CMS at /studio (~45 screens, ~150 API handlers) that no public link points to. Its content does NOT live in files: it lives in Postgres as Page + PageSection rows whose payloads are JSON, rendered through 30+ typed block types, with prisma/seed.ts holding only a three-page first draft (home, about, contact) for a fresh install. Two important qualifications: (a) the demonstration corpus — 42 crafts, 24 people, 14 projects, 30 publications, 13 partners — is opt-in and its PEOPLE, PROJECTS, PUBLICATIONS and PARTNERS are explicitly fictional, while the craft records are real; (b) the single richest body of genuine institutional identity copy (the owner's 16-chapter script: mission, five objectives, three principles, research themes, positioning) is hard-coded in components/sections/story/CinematicScroll.tsx, deliberately NOT in the CMS. My recommendation is a split verdict: lift the identity prose (small, mostly already in code, no database needed) and redirect for everything else — because Portal_Development_Designer today has exactly ONE public page and no content model at all, so "importing the content" is really "rebuilding a finished CMS", and cxa-portal already frames Portal_Development_Designer's product as one of its own three platform pillars ("Field Repositories").

- **Portal_Development_Designer has no public content surface to import anything INTO. There is one public route (/) and no route group for institutional pages.**

  - change here: A new app/(public)/ or app/(site)/ route group beside app/(protected)/, with its own layout.tsx (header/footer/nav), since app/(protected)/layout.tsx is auth-gated and cannot be reused.
  - android twin: NONE — this is web-only marketing surface; the Android app has no public/unauthenticated screens.
- **Portal_Development_Designer has no content model — no Page, no PageSection, no SectionType, no block registry, no rich-text storage. Nothing exists to hold editable institutional copy.**
  - evidence: cxa-portal's model is Page + PageSection with JSON payloads and 37 SectionType values (F:/cxa-portal/prisma/schema.prisma, enum SectionType; F:/cxa-portal/README.md:217-223). No equivalent was found on the Portal_Development_Designer side; its data model is workshop/artisan/product/questionnaire shaped (F:/Portal_Development_Designer/frontend/app/(protected)/).
  - change here: backend/ (FastAPI + its Prisma/SQLAlchemy schema) would need page and section tables plus publish-state filtering; frontend would need a section renderer and per-block Zod schemas. This is the single largest cost item and is why 'import the content' is really 'build a CMS'.
  - android twin: NONE
- **No editorial safety machinery exists to receive imported content: no soft-delete read filters, no revision history, no audit-on-write transaction, no draft-leak check.**
  - evidence: cxa-portal enforces all four (F:/cxa-portal/README.md:227-235 for the invariants; F:/cxa-portal/README.md:304-322 for the leak-check). Its highest-consequence defect class is 'a draft or soft-deleted record appearing on the public site'.
  - change here: Any import must bring lib/content.ts-equivalent filters and an audit wrapper, or accept that a half-written About page can go public. Do not import content without the filters that keep unpublished rows off the public site.
  - android twin: NONE
- **The Centre's real identity copy is NOT in a database and therefore is NOT reachable by any data export — it is hard-coded in one 65 KB React component.**
  - evidence: F:/cxa-portal/components/sections/story/CinematicScroll.tsx:42-45 states the words are code, not CMS fields, on purpose; the objectives/principles/positioning arrays are at lines 430-494.
  - change here: This is good news for lifting: F:/cxa-portal/components/sections/story/CinematicScroll.tsx:421-506 and F:/cxa-portal/components/sections/PlatformPillarsSection.tsx:67-73 can be copied as plain TypeScript constants with no database involved at all. It is bad news for the user's editability goal — this copy is not editable in the existing CMS either.
  - android twin: NONE
- **Two content lists are explicitly NOT yet approved by the institution and must not be imported as fact.**
  - evidence: F:/cxa-portal/components/sections/story/CinematicScroll.tsx:47-49 — '⚠ TWO LISTS STILL NEED AN INSTITUTIONAL DECISION before launch, per the script's own notes: the research themes (trim to the APPROVED scope) and — on the page section that follows — the six audience commitments.' Repeated at line 484 and at F:/cxa-portal/prisma/seed.ts:524.
  - change here: RESEARCH_THEMES (CinematicScroll.tsx:485-494) and the FEATURE_GRID items (seed.ts:532-569). Each is one array; each cut is one line. Get sign-off before publishing either in a second place.
  - android twin: NONE
- **There is no real people/leadership data anywhere to import. The Centre's actual staff have never been entered.**
  - evidence: F:/cxa-portal/prisma/corpus/people.ts:21-23 — every one of the 24 people is invented. F:/cxa-portal/prisma/seed.ts:831-838 seeds the About page's Leadership block with mode 'leadership' and ids: [], i.e. empty. F:/cxa-portal/lib/settings/schema.ts:199 — tagline defaults to empty string.
  - change here: Nothing to lift. If Portal_Development_Designer needs a leadership or people section, the names must be gathered from the Centre directly — copying prisma/corpus/people.ts would publish fictional staff under a real institution's name.
  - android twin: NONE
- **No postal address, telephone or contact email exists in the source to import.**
  - evidence: F:/cxa-portal/lib/settings/schema.ts:393-400 — addressLine1/2, city, state, postalCode all default to ""; only country defaults to 'India'. F:/cxa-portal/prisma/seed.ts:880-893 — the MAP block's address is seeded EMPTY on purpose, with lat/long left at 0,0 which MapSection treats as 'no coordinates set'.
  - change here: The contact facts must come from the Centre. Only the framing prose (seed.ts:852-877) is liftable.
  - android twin: NONE

### Traps and risks

- RECOMMENDATION — SPLIT THE DECISION; do not treat this as one yes/no. (a) LIFT the identity prose: mission, five objectives, three principles, positioning, the three-question framing, the six audiences, the About hero and vision paragraphs, the contact framing, and the five-chapter craft story. That is roughly 250-400 lines of prose, it is genuinely good, it is already partly plain TypeScript constants, and copying it needs NO database and NO CMS. (b) REDIRECT for everything else: crafts, people, news, events, publications, projects, gallery, search, A-Z. Those are ~30 routes backed by ~60 Prisma models, a media pipeline, an audit log and a four-layer verification suite. Rebuilding them in Portal_Development_Designer is not a content migration, it is a second CMS.
- THE STATED INTENT CUTS AGAINST WHAT IS ACTUALLY THERE, and the user should see this before choosing. 'Editability should not remain over there' implies moving editing INTO Portal_Development_Designer. But cxa-portal IS the editing tool — ~45 studio screens, ~150 API handlers, revisions with restore, soft-delete, per-record audit, content locks, a studio access list with a master-admin tier, OAuth, 2FA, presigned media uploads with derivatives, publication scheduling and embargo (F:/cxa-portal/app/api/studio, F:/cxa-portal/README.md:94-133,227-235). Portal_Development_Designer has none of it. Consolidating editing into Portal_Development_Designer means REBUILDING all of that, and the interim state is strictly less editable than today. If the real goal is 'one place to edit', the cheaper direction is the reverse of the one assumed: keep cxa-portal as the CMS and let Portal_Development_Designer link to it.
- THE TWO PRODUCTS ARE ALREADY MODELLED AS SIBLINGS, NOT AS DUPLICATES, which argues for linking. cxa-portal's PLATFORM_PILLARS block names three instruments, and the second is 'FIELD REPOSITORIES' — 'What fieldwork brings home — catalogued with the place, hands and context it came from' (F:/cxa-portal/components/sections/PlatformPillarsSection.tsx:9-16,67-73). Its README calls Portal_Development_Designer's product 'the sibling Field Repository product' and says the visual language is shared verbatim (F:/cxa-portal/README.md:249-251). The existing site already frames this app as one of the Centre's pillars; merging them collapses a deliberate parent/child relationship.
- DO NOT IMPORT THE DEMONSTRATION CORPUS. Its people, projects, publications and partners are explicitly invented (F:/cxa-portal/prisma/corpus/people.ts:21-23). It is opt-in by name in the source repo precisely because 'seeded fiction has a habit of reaching production and being discovered by a visitor rather than by a developer' (F:/cxa-portal/README.md:165-168). A bulk copy into a real product would publish fictional staff under IIT Kharagpur's name. The 42 CRAFT records are the exception — those are real and carefully sourced (F:/cxa-portal/prisma/corpus/crafts.ts:13-19).
- PHOTOGRAPH LICENSING IS LOAD-BEARING AND BREAKS SILENTLY. The 26 bundled craft photographs are mostly CC BY / BY-SA, which grant publication rights ONLY on the condition that attribution travels with the work. cxa-portal enforces this structurally: CraftPhoto renders ImageCredit unconditionally, and /credits lists every one (F:/cxa-portal/README.md:178-187). Copying the image files without also porting the credit component and the licence manifest (F:/cxa-portal/lib/media/craft-imagery.ts) is a licence violation that no test will catch.
- SEED LITERALS ARE NOT WHAT THE LIVE SITE SHOWS. Everything I quoted from prisma/seed.ts is a FRESH-INSTALL first draft. The seed deliberately steps over rows that already exist, and the file documents by measurement that a seeded database was still serving older placeholder text long after these literals were corrected (F:/cxa-portal/prisma/seed.ts:419-428, 677-688). The About copy carries its own warning: '⚠ THE COPY BELOW IS A FRESH INSTALLATION'S FIRST DRAFT, NOT THE CENTRE'S FINAL WORDS' (F:/cxa-portal/prisma/seed.ts:690-694). Before lifting any of it as final, read the actual database rows, not this file.
- UNVERIFIED — THE F: DRIVE WENT OFFLINE MID-RECONNAISSANCE and did not return within ~2 minutes of polling. It is a 1 TB USB Mass Storage Device (Get-CimInstance Win32_DiskDrive) hosting BOTH cxa-portal and Portal_Development_Designer, and its flakiness caused every command timeout in this session. Four things I intended to check and could not: (1) cxa-portal's git history and last commit date, so I cannot say how actively it is maintained; (2) middleware.ts (8,438 bytes) — I have only the README's description of its /studio/* matcher; (3) the docs/ set (ARCHITECTURE 55 KB, CONTRACT 30 KB, DATA-MODEL 29 KB, OUTSTANDING 36 KB), which likely contains further decisions relevant to this call; (4) whether a production deployment is currently serving — .env.example:90 sets NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL to localhost:3000, so I have NO verified public URL for the existing site. That last point matters directly: 'redirect out to the existing site' presumes a live address I could not confirm exists.
- IF CONSOLIDATION INTO Portal_Development_Designer IS NON-NEGOTIABLE, scope it as three separate pieces rather than one migration: (1) static identity pages — about, mission, contact — as plain React with copy lifted from the paths above, no CMS, shippable in days; (2) a minimal Page/PageSection model plus a section renderer if those pages must be editable by non-developers, which is weeks and needs the soft-delete and audit filters ported with it; (3) crafts/news/events/people/publications, which should stay in cxa-portal and be linked to. Doing (1) alone already removes the 'the Centre's story lives somewhere else' problem for most visitors, at a fraction of the cost, and leaves (2) and (3) as decisions that can be taken later with better information.
